## Transcript scope and redaction

This file is exported from the Claude Code session log and contains the S1-011 portion of the
session, starting at the request to begin the story: prompts, assistant messages, and tool calls with
their output. The earlier exchange in the same session, which only set this per-feature transcript
preference, is omitted. Model reasoning and injected system context are not part of the conversation
and are not included. Re-export before committing if the session continues.

Local filesystem paths, the local username, the author's email address, and session identifiers are
replaced with bracketed redactions. No project credentials, Supabase keys, database passwords, or
access tokens appeared in the source log; the Supabase values shown are local dummy test values.

````text
[﻿story_id]
S1-011

[title]
S1-011 - Associate User-Owned Data with Authenticated Identity

[body]
**Outcome:**
When an authenticated user creates a user-owned ReviewLens record, ownership is derived from the verified authenticated identity and persisted with the record.

**Technical Guidance:**

The backend must derive ownership from trusted authentication context.

For example, a request such as:

`POST /analysis-targets`

must not become owned by another user merely because the client submits a different `userId`.

The exact persistence design is the team's decision. Acceptable approaches include:

1. Provider user identifier stored as owner.
2. Internal User entity mapped to provider identity.
3. Database-level tenant identity derived from authenticated context.

Whatever approach is selected must be applied consistently to user-scoped records.

**Rules:** S1-BR-005, S1-BR-007, S1-BR-010

# `src/services` — Server-side business logic

Domain logic and data access live here, not in React components or directly in Route Handlers.
Services are server-only: never import them from a Client Component (`"use client"`).

Planned folders (created by the stories that need them):

| Folder              | Responsibility                                     | Stories                 |
| ------------------- | -------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------- |
| `auth/`             | Resolve the verified user from the session         | S1-009 – S1-011         |
| `analysis-targets/` | Create, list, and authorize AnalysisTargets        | S1-012, S1-014 – S1-018 |
| `ingestion/`        | Fetch reviews from the source URL, record attempts | S1-019, S1-020, S1-023  |
| `reviews/`          | Normalize and persist Reviews                      | S1-013, S1-021, S1-022  |

Every service that touches user-owned data must take the authenticated user's identity as input
and enforce ownership itself (S1-BR-007 through S1-BR-010).
````

````text
=== repository
import type { SupabaseClient } from "@supabase/supabase-js";

// S1-016 (#32) defines the same platform value in src/lib/validation/analysis-target.ts.
// Import it from there once #32 is merged so there is a single definition.
export type ReviewPlatform = "google_maps";

export interface AnalysisTarget {
  id: string;
  ownerId: string;
  name: string;
  platform: ReviewPlatform;
  sourceUrl: string;
  /** Google place ID resolved during ingestion (S1-020); null until then. */
  googlePlaceId: string | null;
  createdAt: string;
}

/** Already-validated target fields. Ownership is never part of this input. */
export interface NewAnalysisTarget {
  name: string;
  platform: ReviewPlatform;
  sourceUrl: string;
  googlePlaceId?: string;
}

interface AnalysisTargetRow {
  id: string;
  owner_id: string;
  name: string;
  platform: ReviewPlatform;
  source_url: string;
  google_place_id: string | null;
  created_at: string;
}

const TABLE = "analysis_targets";
const COLUMNS = "id, owner_id, name, platform, source_url, google_place_id, created_at";
const UUID_PATTERN = /^[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}$/i;

export class AnalysisTargetPersistenceError extends Error {
  readonly code = "ANALYSIS_TARGET_PERSISTENCE_FAILED";

  constructor(operation: "create" | "get", options?: { cause?: unknown }) {
    // The message is safe to show; database details stay on `cause` for server-side logging.
    super(`Could not ${operation} the analysis target.`, options);
    this.name = "AnalysisTargetPersistenceError";
  }
}

function toAnalysisTarget(row: AnalysisTargetRow): AnalysisTarget {
  return {
    id: row.id,
    ownerId: row.owner_id,
    name: row.name,
    platform: row.platform,
    sourceUrl: row.source_url,
    googlePlaceId: row.google_place_id,
    createdAt: row.created_at,
  };
}

/**
 * Persists a new AnalysisTarget owned by `ownerId`.
 *
 * `ownerId` must come from the verified session (S1-011), never from request input. The client
 * must be a user-scoped Supabase client so Row Level Security also checks ownership.
 */
export async function createAnalysisTarget(
  client: SupabaseClient,
  ownerId: string,
  input: NewAnalysisTarget,
): Promise<AnalysisTarget> {
  const { data, error } = await client
    .from(TABLE)
    .insert({
      owner_id: ownerId,
      name: input.name,
      platform: input.platform,
      source_url: input.sourceUrl,
      google_place_id: input.googlePlaceId ?? null,
    })
    .select(COLUMNS)
    .single<AnalysisTargetRow>();

  if (error || !data) {
    throw new AnalysisTargetPersistenceError("create", { cause: error });
  }

  return toAnalysisTarget(data);
}

/**
 * Returns the target with `targetId` if it belongs to `ownerId`, otherwise `null`.
 *
 * Missing and foreign targets both return `null` so callers can respond with 404 without
 * revealing that another user's target exists.
 */
export async function getAnalysisTargetById(
  client: SupabaseClient,
  ownerId: string,
  targetId: string,
): Promise<AnalysisTarget | null> {
  if (!UUID_PATTERN.test(targetId)) {
    return null;
  }

  const { data, error } = await client
    .from(TABLE)
    .select(COLUMNS)
    .eq("id", targetId)
    .eq("owner_id", ownerId)
    .maybeSingle<AnalysisTargetRow>();

  if (error) {
    throw new AnalysisTargetPersistenceError("get", { cause: error });
  }

  return data ? toAnalysisTarget(data) : null;
}
=== migration
-- S1-014: AnalysisTarget persistence model.
--
-- One row per business or item a user wants to analyze. Every target belongs to exactly one
-- authenticated user (S1-003, engineering standards section 8). Reviews and ingestion attempts
-- will reference analysis_targets.id in later stories, so a target can have many of each.

create table public.analysis_targets (
  id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid(),
  owner_id uuid not null references auth.users (id) on delete cascade,
  name text not null,
  platform text not null,
  source_url text not null,
  google_place_id text,
  created_at timestamptz not null default now(),

  -- Mirrors the S1-016 validation rules so the database rejects bad rows even if a caller
  -- skips validation.
  constraint analysis_targets_name_present check (char_length(btrim(name)) > 0),
  constraint analysis_targets_name_length check (char_length(name) <= 120),
  constraint analysis_targets_platform_supported check (platform in ('google_maps')),
  constraint analysis_targets_source_url_http check (source_url ~* '^https?://'),
  constraint analysis_targets_google_place_id_present check (
    google_place_id is null or char_length(btrim(google_place_id)) > 0
  )
);

comment on table public.analysis_targets is
  'Businesses or items a user analyzes. Owned by exactly one auth.users row.';
comment on column public.analysis_targets.platform is
  'Supported review platform. Sprint 1 supports google_maps only.';
comment on column public.analysis_targets.google_place_id is
  'Optional Google place ID resolved during ingestion (S1-020), reused so later imports skip '
  'resolving the business again. Not unique: different users may analyze the same business.';

-- Supports listing a user's targets newest first (S1-017).
create index analysis_targets_owner_id_created_at_idx
  on public.analysis_targets (owner_id, created_at desc);

-- Row Level Security: a signed-in user can only see and change their own targets (S1-BR-008).
-- Server code must still check ownership itself; RLS is defense in depth.
alter table public.analysis_targets enable row level security;

revoke all on table public.analysis_targets from anon;
grant select, insert, update, delete on table public.analysis_targets to authenticated;

create policy analysis_targets_select_own
  on public.analysis_targets
  for select
  to authenticated
  using ((select auth.uid()) = owner_id);

create policy analysis_targets_insert_own
  on public.analysis_targets
  for insert
  to authenticated
  with check ((select auth.uid()) = owner_id);

create policy analysis_targets_update_own
  on public.analysis_targets
  for update
  to authenticated
  using ((select auth.uid()) = owner_id)
  with check ((select auth.uid()) = owner_id);

create policy analysis_targets_delete_own
  on public.analysis_targets
  for delete
  to authenticated
  using ((select auth.uid()) = owner_id);
=== supabase README
# `src/lib/supabase` — Persistent storage boundary

This folder is the single place where the application connects to Supabase (PostgreSQL and Auth).
No other code should construct a Supabase client directly.

Status: **reserved, not implemented.** The application currently has no database connection and
needs no credentials to run.

Planned contents:

- Browser and server Supabase client factories (S1-009).
- Configuration read from environment variables — never hard-coded (S1-008).

Related boundaries:

- Connection settings: `.env.example` lists the variable names; real values go in `.env.local`,
  which Git ignores. S1-008 owns the final variable list and startup validation.
- Schema and migrations: `supabase/migrations/` at the repository root (the Supabase CLI
  default). S1-014 added `analysis_targets`; `npm run test:db` checks migrations and RLS.
- The Supabase service-role key bypasses Row Level Security. It is server-only and must not be
  used for normal user requests (see the S1-003 data and security guardrails).
````

````text
# S1-010: Protected routes and backend operations

S1-010 depends on the S1-009 authentication branch. The PM has created the shared Supabase project.
The user reports successful live registration/confirmation, sign-in, navigation/reload persistence,
session API identity, logout, incorrect-password rejection, and two distinct account identities.

## Boundaries implemented here

- The existing single Proxy refreshes sessions for `/login`, `/auth/*`, `/api/auth/*`,
  `/analysis-targets/*`, and `/api/analysis-targets/*` (including each namespace root).
- For `/analysis-targets` pages, verified SDK claims permit navigation. Missing/invalid identity
  redirects with HTTP 303 to `/login?next=...`; configuration/provider failures return a safe 503.
  Redirects retain SDK cookie writes and private cache headers. API requests continue to their
  handlers; they do not redirect to HTML login pages.
- Login accepts a return destination only inside `/analysis-targets`. Sign-in returns there;
  external URLs, unrelated namespaces, and malformed destinations are ignored. Registration that
  needs email confirmation remains on login. Confirmation links retain S1-009's fixed destination;
  after confirmation users can revisit the protected page to resume navigation.
- `requireApiUser()` independently calls S1-009's server-verified `getAuthenticatedUser()` on each
  operation. It returns either a verified user or a private 401/503 response with no protected data.
````

167	=== access-control
168	import "server-only";
169	
170	import { redirect } from "next/navigation";
171	import { connection, NextResponse } from "next/server";
172	import { getProtectedReturnTo } from "@/lib/validation/return-to";
173	import type { AuthIdentity } from "@/lib/validation/auth";
174	import { getAuthenticatedUser } from "@/services/auth/auth-service";
175	
176	// Call inside the protected page/data operation, behind Suspense. A layout
177	// check alone cannot protect independently rendered children or Server Actions.
178	export async function requirePageUser(returnTo?: string): Promise<AuthIdentity> {
179	  await connection();
180	  const user = await getAuthenticatedUser();
181	  if (!user) {
182	    const destination = getProtectedReturnTo(returnTo);
183	    redirect(destination ? `/login?next=${encodeURIComponent(destination)}` : "/login");
184	  }
185	  return user;
186	}
187	
188	type ApiAuthentication =
189	  { user: AuthIdentity; response?: never } | { user?: never; response: NextResponse };
190	
191	// Return immediately when response is present. Only then call a resource
192	// service with user.id; S1-011 owns the resource ownership checks.
193	export async function requireApiUser(): Promise<ApiAuthentication> {
194	  await connection();
195	  try {
196	    const user = await getAuthenticatedUser();
197	    if (user) return { user };
198	    return { response: authFailureResponse(false) };
199	  } catch {
200	    return { response: authFailureResponse(true) };
201	  }
202	}
203	
204	function authFailureResponse(unavailable: boolean) {
205	  return NextResponse.json(
206	    {
207	      success: false,
208	      error: unavailable
209	        ? {
210	            code: "AUTH_UNAVAILABLE",
211	            message: "Authentication is temporarily unavailable. Please try again later.",
212	          }
213	        : { code: "AUTH_REQUIRED", message: "Please sign in to continue." },
214	    },
215	    { status: unavailable ? 503 : 401, headers: { "Cache-Control": "private, no-store" } },
216	  );
217	}

261	=== PR38
262	feature/S1-014-analysis-target-model
263	## Summary
264	
265	Adds the independently testable S1-012 authorization scenario on top of S1-014's existing AnalysisTarget persistence model.
266	
267	- Exercises User B's direct known-ID read, update, and delete attempts against User A's target at the real PostgreSQL/RLS boundary.
268	- Switches back to User A and verifies that the complete stored row still exists and every value is unchanged after both unauthorized mutation attempts.
269	- Covers an authenticated database role with no verified user identity and the `anon` role.
270	- Confirms User A can update and delete the same owned target, proving the denial is ownership-scoped.
271	- Reuses S1-014's migration, policies, auth shim, and database runner; no RLS policy, repository, authentication, API, or UI code is duplicated.
272	
273	Refs #12
274	
275	## Existing S1-014 protection
276	
277	PR #35 already provides:
278	
279	- `owner_id not null` with an `auth.users` foreign key.
280	- RLS policies for own-row select, insert, update, and delete.
281	- No table access for `anon`.
282	- A repository read filtered by both target ID and server-provided owner ID, returning `null` for missing and foreign targets.
283	- Creation that derives `owner_id` from a separate trusted service argument and ignores owner fields smuggled into input.
284	- User A/User B database tests for hidden cross-user rows, zero-row cross-user updates/deletes, owner spoofing, owner reassignment, missing identity, and anonymous denial.
285	
286	## Gap addressed
287	
288	S1-014 asserted that User B's update and delete affected zero rows, but did not subsequently switch back to User A and independently verify that Target A remained present with all stored fields unchanged. This PR adds that explicit victim-state invariant without modifying Aryan's implementation.
289	
290	## Acceptance criteria and automated coverage
291	
292	- [x] User A owns and retrieves Target A by its deterministic ID.
293	- [x] User B deliberately queries that known ID and receives no row or protected data.
294	- [x] User B's update and delete attempts against that ID affect zero rows.
295	- [x] User A subsequently retrieves Target A.
296	- [x] Owner, name, platform, source URL, Google place ID, and creation timestamp remain unchanged.
297	- [x] A request with no verified user ID cannot read, update, or delete Target A.
298	- [x] The `anon` role is rejected at the table boundary.
299	- [x] User A can update and delete the owned target.
300	- [x] The test uses deterministic independent identities and rolls its fixture back.
301	
302	Test: `tests/db/cross-user-analysis-target-authorization.test.sql`
303	
304	## Security explanation
305	
306	This test targets PostgreSQL with RLS enabled, not a UI mock. For a foreign target ID, User B sees the same zero-row behavior as a missing ID, so the test does not disclose the target's existence or content. S1-014's separate tests continue to prove that client-supplied owner IDs cannot establish ownership.
307	
308	This authorization/database change requires the normal teammate review plus the additional security-focused reviewer required by `docs/AI-Assisted-Development.md`.
309

311

315	- `npm run format:check`
316	- `npm run lint`
317	- `npm run typecheck`

371	## Summary
372	
373	Implements the independent Create Analysis Target UI for S1-015 on top of the existing S1-016 validator.
374	
375	- Adds the responsive `/analysis-targets/new` route and accessible target form.
376	- Uses `validateAnalysisTargetInput()` for fast client-side validation without introducing a second validation schema.
377	- Adds required indicators, inline errors, first-invalid-field focus, preserved input, loading state, duplicate-submit protection, Cancel, success confirmation, and workspace navigation after a successful creation response.
378	- Adds a typed browser creation client that sends only `name` and `sourceUrl`; ownership is intentionally absent from the request.
379	- Initializes the repository's required shadcn/ui convention and adds only the primitives used by this form.
380	- Documents the future authenticated Route Handler and S1-014 service integration contract.
381	- Adds deterministic UI/client tests. Mocked success verifies UI behavior only and is not claimed as Supabase persistence.

**Tool: Bash** — Create S1-011 branch from S1-010 and merge S1-014

IN

````text
cd "[REDACTED LOCAL REPOSITORY PATH]" && git fetch origin feature/S1-016-target-input-validation:refs/remotes/origin/feature/S1-016-target-input-validation 2>&1 | tail -1
git switch -c feature/S1-011-owned-records origin/feature/S1-010-protected-routes 2>&1 | tail -1
git merge --no-edit origin/feature/S1-014-analysis-target-model 2>&1 | tail -6; echo "--- status after 014"; git status --short | grep -E '^(UU|AA|DU|UD|AU|UA|DD)' 
echo "--- in merge? $(git rev-parse -q --verify MERGE_HEAD)"
````


````text
++<<<<<<< HEAD
 +Authentication specifications live under `tests/e2e/auth/` and run separately with
 +`npm run test:e2e:auth`, which starts a local dummy provider and an isolated app instance.
 +The smoke command excludes those tests so it does not require the provider. Neither auth test
 +accounts nor live Supabase credentials are needed. Both suites can use an existing supported
 +browser by setting `PLAYWRIGHT_CHANNEL` (for example, `msedge`); otherwise they use bundled Chromium.
++=======
+ ### Run database tests
+ 
+ `npm run test:db` checks the real schema and Row Level Security policies, not a mock. It creates a
+ throwaway PostgreSQL database, applies a small test-only stand-in for Supabase Auth
+ (`tests/db/supabase-auth-shim.sql`), applies every migration in `supabase/migrations/`, runs each
+ `tests/db/*.test.sql` file, and drops the database. The tests use two users, A and B, to prove
+ cross-user access is denied at the database.
+ 
+ It needs PostgreSQL 15 or newer running locally (macOS: `brew install postgresql@16` and
+ `brew services start postgresql@16`). Connection settings come from the standard `PG*` variables.
+ CI runs them in the "Database tests" job against a PostgreSQL 16 container.
````

````text
== node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/03-api-reference/03-file-conventions/route-groups.md
---
title: Route Groups
description: Route Groups can be used to partition your Next.js application into different sections.
---

Route Groups are a folder convention that let you organize routes by category or team.

## Convention

A route group can be created by wrapping a folder's name in parenthesis: `(folderName)`.

This convention indicates the folder is for organizational purposes and should **not be included** in the route's URL path.

<Image
  alt="An example folder structure using route groups"
  srcLight="/docs/light/project-organization-route-groups.png"
  srcDark="/docs/dark/project-organization-route-groups.png"
  width="1600"
  height="849"
/>

## Use cases

- Organizing routes by team, concern, or feature.
- Defining multiple [root layouts](/docs/app/api-reference/file-conventions/layout#root-layout).
- Opting specific route segments into sharing a layout, while keeping others out.

## Caveats

- **Full page load**: If you navigate between routes that use different root layouts, it'll trigger a full page reload. For example, navigating from `/cart` that uses `app/(shop)/layout.js` to `/blog` that uses `app/(marketing)/layout.js`. This **only** applies to multiple root layouts.
- **Conflicting paths**: Routes in different groups should not resolve to the same URL path. For example, `(marketing)/about/page.js` and `(shop)/about/page.js` would both resolve to `/about` and cause an error.
- **Top-level root layout**: If you use multiple root layouts without a top-level `layout.js` file, make sure your home route (/) is defined within one of the route groups, e.g. app/(marketing)/page.js.
=== server.ts
import "server-only";

import { createServerClient, type CookieMethodsServer } from "@supabase/ssr";
import { cookies } from "next/headers";
import { getSupabasePublicConfig } from "@/lib/supabase/config";

/** Use writable only in Server Actions or Route Handlers, never during rendering. */
export async function createSupabaseServerClient(writable = false) {
  const { url, publishableKey } = getSupabasePublicConfig();
  const cookieStore = await cookies();

  const adapter: CookieMethodsServer = { getAll: () => cookieStore.getAll() };
  if (writable) {
    adapter.setAll = (cookiesToSet) => {
      for (const { name, value, options } of cookiesToSet) {
        cookieStore.set(name, value, options);
      }
    };
  }
  return createServerClient(url, publishableKey, { cookies: adapter });
}
=== auth-service
import "server-only";

import { isAuthSessionMissingError } from "@supabase/supabase-js";
import { createSupabaseServerClient } from "@/lib/supabase/server";
import { authCredentialsSchema, type AuthIdentity, type AuthResult } from "@/lib/validation/auth";

const unavailableMessage = "Authentication is temporarily unavailable. Please try again later.";
const invalidSessionCodes = new Set([
  "bad_jwt",
  "user_not_found",
  "session_not_found",
  "session_expired",
  "refresh_token_not_found",
  "refresh_token_already_used",
  "user_banned",
]);

function logFailure(operation: string) {
  // Deliberately omit provider errors, credentials, cookies, and user identifiers.
  console.warn("Authentication operation failed", { operation });
}

export async function getAuthenticatedUser(): Promise<AuthIdentity | null> {
  try {
    const supabase = await createSupabaseServerClient();
    // getUser verifies with Supabase Auth; cookie contents alone are never identity.
    const { data, error } = await supabase.auth.getUser();
    if (error) {
      if (
        isAuthSessionMissingError(error) ||
        error.status === 401 ||
        error.status === 403 ||
        (error.code && invalidSessionCodes.has(error.code))
      )
        return null;
      throw new Error(unavailableMessage);
    }
    return data.user ? { id: data.user.id, email: data.user.email ?? null } : null;
  } catch {
    logFailure("current-user");
    throw new Error(unavailableMessage);
  }
}

export async function authenticate(
  input: unknown,
  mode: "sign-in" | "register",
): Promise<AuthResult> {
  const parsed = authCredentialsSchema.safeParse(input);
  if (!parsed.success) {
    const fieldErrors: AuthResult["fieldErrors"] = {};
    for (const issue of parsed.error.issues) {
      const field = issue.path[0];
      if ((field === "email" || field === "password") && !fieldErrors[field]) {
        fieldErrors[field] = issue.message;
      }
    }
    return { success: false, message: "Please fix the highlighted fields.", fieldErrors };
  }

  try {
    const supabase = await createSupabaseServerClient(true);
    const { data, error } =
      mode === "register"
        ? await supabase.auth.signUp(parsed.data)
        : await supabase.auth.signInWithPassword(parsed.data);

    if (error) {
      logFailure(mode);
      const message =
        error.status && error.status >= 500
          ? unavailableMessage
          : error.status === 429
            ? "Too many attempts. Please wait a moment and try again."
            : mode === "register"
              ? "We couldn't create your account. Check your details and the password requirements, then try again."
              : "We couldn't sign you in. Check your email and password and try again.";
      return { success: false, message };
    }
    if (mode === "register" && !data.session) {
      return {
        success: true,
        requiresConfirmation: true,
        message:
          "Check your email for a confirmation link. After confirming, return here to sign in.",
      };
    }
    if (!data.session || !data.user) return { success: false, message: unavailableMessage };
    return { success: true };
  } catch {
    logFailure(mode);
    return { success: false, message: unavailableMessage };
  }
}

export async function endSession(): Promise<AuthResult> {
  try {
    const supabase = await createSupabaseServerClient(true);
    const { error } = await supabase.auth.signOut({ scope: "local" });
    if (error) {
      logFailure("sign-out");
      return { success: false, message: "We couldn't sign you out. Please try again." };
    }
    return { success: true };
  } catch {
    logFailure("sign-out");
    return { success: false, message: unavailableMessage };
  }
}

export async function confirmEmail(tokenHash: string): Promise<boolean> {
  try {
    const supabase = await createSupabaseServerClient(true);
    const { data, error } = await supabase.auth.verifyOtp({ token_hash: tokenHash, type: "email" });
    if (error || !data.session) {
      logFailure("confirm-email");
      return false;
    }
    return true;
  } catch {
    logFailure("confirm-email");
    return false;
  }
}
=== validation/auth
import { z } from "zod";

export const authCredentialsSchema = z.object({
  email: z
    .string()
    .trim()
    .min(1, "Enter your email address.")
    .email("Enter a valid email address."),
  // Passwords must reach the provider exactly as entered, including whitespace.
  // Supabase owns password policy; do not impose a different login policy here.
  password: z.string().min(1, "Enter your password."),
});

export type AuthCredentials = z.infer<typeof authCredentialsSchema>;
export type AuthResult = {
  success: boolean;
  message?: string;
  requiresConfirmation?: boolean;
  fieldErrors?: Partial<Record<keyof AuthCredentials, string>>;
};

export type AuthIdentity = { id: string; email: string | null };

import { NextResponse } from "next/server";
import { requireApiUser } from "@/services/auth/access-control";

export async function GET() {
  const auth = await requireApiUser();
  if (auth.response) return auth.response;
  return NextResponse.json(
    { success: true, data: { user: auth.user } },
    { headers: { "Cache-Control": "private, no-store" } },
  );
}
=== session route test
/** @jest-environment node */
import { GET } from "@/app/api/auth/session/route";
import { getAuthenticatedUser } from "@/services/auth/auth-service";

jest.mock("@/services/auth/auth-service", () => ({ getAuthenticatedUser: jest.fn() }));
jest.mock("next/server", () => ({
  ...jest.requireActual("next/server"),
  connection: jest.fn().mockResolvedValue(undefined),
}));

it("returns only server-verified identity in the shared response shape", async () => {
  const user = { id: "verified-user", email: "test@example.com" };
  jest.mocked(getAuthenticatedUser).mockResolvedValue(user);
  const response = await GET();
  expect(response.status).toBe(200);
  expect(response.headers.get("Cache-Control")).toContain("no-store");
  await expect(response.json()).resolves.toEqual({ success: true, data: { user } });
});
it("rejects missing identity without returning user data", async () => {
  jest.mocked(getAuthenticatedUser).mockResolvedValue(null);
  const response = await GET();
  expect(response.status).toBe(401);
  await expect(response.json()).resolves.toEqual({
    success: false,
    error: { code: "AUTH_REQUIRED", message: "Please sign in to continue." },
  });
});
it("returns a safe unavailable response for provider failure", async () => {
  jest
    .mocked(getAuthenticatedUser)
    .mockRejectedValue(new Error("private-key-and-provider-details"));
  const response = await GET();
  expect(response.status).toBe(503);
  const body = await response.text();
  expect(body).toContain("AUTH_UNAVAILABLE");
  expect(body).not.toContain("private-key");
});

=== S1-015 API client
import type { AnalysisTarget, CreateAnalysisTarget } from "@/types/analysis-target";
import type { AnalysisTargetValidationIssue } from "@/lib/validation/analysis-target";

type AnalysisTargetCreationErrorCode =
  | "AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED"
  | "INVALID_ANALYSIS_TARGET_INPUT"
  | "CREATION_UNAVAILABLE"
  | "CREATION_FAILED";

interface ErrorPayload {
  success: false;
  error: {
    code?: unknown;
    message?: unknown;
    issues?: unknown;
  };
}

export class AnalysisTargetCreationError extends Error {
  constructor(
    public readonly code: AnalysisTargetCreationErrorCode,
    message: string,
    public readonly issues: readonly AnalysisTargetValidationIssue[] = [],
  ) {
    super(message);
    this.name = "AnalysisTargetCreationError";
  }
}

function isRecord(value: unknown): value is Record<string, unknown> {
  return typeof value === "object" && value !== null && !Array.isArray(value);
}

function isErrorPayload(value: unknown): value is ErrorPayload {
  return isRecord(value) && value.success === false && isRecord(value.error);
}

function isValidationIssue(value: unknown): value is AnalysisTargetValidationIssue {
  if (!isRecord(value) || typeof value.code !== "string" || typeof value.message !== "string") {
    return false;
  }

  return value.field === undefined || value.field === "name" || value.field === "sourceUrl";
}

function readValidationIssues(value: unknown): readonly AnalysisTargetValidationIssue[] {
  return Array.isArray(value) && value.every(isValidationIssue) ? value : [];
}

function isAnalysisTarget(value: unknown): value is AnalysisTarget {
  return (
    isRecord(value) &&
    typeof value.id === "string" &&
    value.id.length > 0 &&
    typeof value.name === "string" &&
    typeof value.sourceUrl === "string" &&
    value.platform === "google_maps"
  );
}

async function readJson(response: Response): Promise<unknown> {
  try {
    return await response.json();
  } catch {
    return undefined;
  }
}

/**
 * Browser-side client for the future authenticated S1-009/S1-014 creation endpoint.
 * Ownership is intentionally absent from the request body and must be derived by the server.
 */
export const createAnalysisTarget: CreateAnalysisTarget = async ({ name, sourceUrl }) => {
  let response: Response;

  try {
    response = await fetch("/api/analysis-targets", {
      method: "POST",
      headers: { "content-type": "application/json" },
      body: JSON.stringify({ name, sourceUrl }),
    });
  } catch {
    throw new AnalysisTargetCreationError(
      "CREATION_UNAVAILABLE",
      "We couldn't reach ReviewLens. Check your connection and try again.",
    );
  }

  const payload = await readJson(response);

  if (response.status === 401) {
    throw new AnalysisTargetCreationError(
      "AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED",
      "Your session has expired. Please sign in again.",
    );
  }

  if (response.status === 400 && isErrorPayload(payload)) {
    throw new AnalysisTargetCreationError(
      "INVALID_ANALYSIS_TARGET_INPUT",
      typeof payload.error.message === "string"
        ? payload.error.message
        : "Please fix the highlighted fields.",
      readValidationIssues(payload.error.issues),
    );
  }

  if (!response.ok) {
    throw new AnalysisTargetCreationError(
      response.status === 404 ? "CREATION_UNAVAILABLE" : "CREATION_FAILED",
      response.status === 404
        ? "Target creation is temporarily unavailable. Please try again later."
        : "We couldn't create this target. Please try again.",
    );
  }

  if (!isRecord(payload) || payload.success !== true || !isAnalysisTarget(payload.data)) {
    throw new AnalysisTargetCreationError(
      "CREATION_FAILED",
      "ReviewLens returned an unexpected response. Please try again.",
    );
  }

  return payload.data;
};
# AnalysisTarget creation integration contract

S1-015 intentionally does not implement authentication (S1-009) or persistence (S1-014).
The client form submits only `name` and `sourceUrl` to `POST /api/analysis-targets`; it never sends
an owner or user identifier.

When the dependent stories are available, the protected Route Handler must:

1. Resolve and verify the Supabase session. Return `401` before any write when no authenticated
   identity exists.
2. Parse the JSON body and call `validateAnalysisTargetInput()` from
   `src/lib/validation/analysis-target.ts`. Return its structured error as `400` before calling
   persistence.
3. Pass only the validated `name`, `sourceUrl`, and fixed `platform`, plus the server-derived user
   identity, to the S1-014 service.
4. Return `201` as `{ success: true, data: AnalysisTarget }` after a genuine committed write.
5. Translate expected failures to the shared safe error response and never return fabricated target
   data.

The S1-014 result must expose at least `id`, `name`, `sourceUrl`, and `platform` so the client can
navigate to `/analysis-targets/{id}?created=1`. The workspace should use that query marker to show
the standards-required confirmation: “Target created. You can now start ingestion.”
=== S1-015 story
**Outcome:**
An authenticated user can create a new AnalysisTarget from the ReviewLens user interface.

**Technical Guidance:**

The application must collect enough information to satisfy the AnalysisTarget persistence model.

Because each team supports only one review platform, the platform may be:

1. Explicitly selected in the UI, or
2. Fixed by team configuration and displayed to the user.

The backend must assign ownership from authenticated identity.

After successful creation, the user should receive clear confirmation and be able to continue working with the newly created target.

Tests should verify both UI/service behavior and persistence where appropriate.

**Rules:** S1-BR-012 through S1-BR-017

/** @jest-environment node */

import type { SupabaseClient } from "@supabase/supabase-js";

import {
  AnalysisTargetPersistenceError,
  createAnalysisTarget,
  getAnalysisTargetById,
} from "@/services/analysis-targets/analysis-target-repository";

const USER_A = "11111111-1111-4111-8111-111111111111";
const TARGET_ID = "33333333-3333-4333-8333-333333333333";

const storedRow = {
  id: TARGET_ID,
  owner_id: USER_A,
  name: "Joe's Pizza",
  platform: "google_maps",
  source_url: "https://www.google.com/maps/place/Joe's+Pizza",
  google_place_id: null,
  created_at: "2026-11-03T12:00:00.000Z",
};

type QueryResult = { data: unknown; error: unknown };

// Records each builder call so tests can assert the exact query sent to Supabase.
function createFakeClient(result: QueryResult) {
  const calls: Array<[string, ...unknown[]]> = [];
  const builder = {
    insert: (...args: unknown[]) => (calls.push(["insert", ...args]), builder),
    select: (...args: unknown[]) => (calls.push(["select", ...args]), builder),
    eq: (...args: unknown[]) => (calls.push(["eq", ...args]), builder),
    single: async () => (calls.push(["single"]), result),
    maybeSingle: async () => (calls.push(["maybeSingle"]), result),
  };
  const client = {
    from: (table: string) => (calls.push(["from", table]), builder),
  };

  return { client: client as unknown as SupabaseClient, calls };
=== api README
# `src/app/api` — Backend Route Handlers

Everything under this folder runs on the server. Each `route.ts` file becomes an HTTP endpoint;
for example `src/app/api/health/route.ts` serves `GET /api/health`.

Route Handlers stay thin (see `docs/engineering-standards.md`, sections 3 and 7):

1. Validate the request.
2. Authenticate the user from the verified Supabase session — never from a client-supplied ID.
3. Authorize access to the requested resource.
4. Call a service in `src/services/`.
5. Return the shared response shape: `{ "success": true, "data": ... }` or
   `{ "success": false, "error": { "code": ..., "message": ... } }`.

Current endpoints:

| Method | Path                | Purpose                                                      | Added by   |
| ------ | ------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------- |
| GET    | `/api/health`       | Confirms the server is running. No auth, no database.        | S1-005     |
| GET    | `/api/auth/session` | Returns verified identity; rejects missing/invalid sessions. | S1-009/010 |

Target, ingestion, and review endpoints arrive with their respective stories. Every protected
handler must independently use `requireApiUser()` from `@/services/auth/access-control` and return
its failure `response` before invoking services. Proxy refreshes cookies but does not guard APIs.
See [S1-010 integration guidance](../../../docs/s1-010-route-protection.md) for ownership handoff
and private response caching.
````

OUT

````text
const MAX_TARGET_NAME_LENGTH = 120;
const MAX_SOURCE_URL_LENGTH = 2_048;

const TARGET_NAME_REQUIRED_MESSAGE = "Enter a name for this target.";
const TARGET_NAME_INVALID_CHARACTERS_MESSAGE = "Target names can't contain control characters.";
const TARGET_NAME_TOO_LONG_MESSAGE = "Target names can be up to 120 characters.";
const SOURCE_URL_REQUIRED_MESSAGE = "Enter the Google Maps link for this target.";
const SOURCE_URL_INVALID_MESSAGE =
  "That doesn't look like a valid web address. Paste the full link, starting with https://.";
const SOURCE_URL_TOO_LONG_MESSAGE = "Google Maps links can be up to 2,048 characters.";
const SOURCE_URL_UNSUPPORTED_PLATFORM_MESSAGE =
  "This link isn't a supported Google Maps link. Open the business in Google Maps and copy its full URL.";
const SOURCE_URL_MISSING_BUSINESS_MESSAGE =
  "This Google Maps link doesn't identify a business. Open a specific business and copy its link.";
const SOURCE_URL_SHORT_LINK_UNSUPPORTED_MESSAGE =
  "Shortened Google Maps links aren't supported yet. Open the full Google Maps URL in your browser and copy it from the address bar.";

const GOOGLE_MAPS_HOSTNAMES = new Set(["google.com", "www.google.com", "maps.google.com"]);
const SHORTENED_GOOGLE_MAPS_HOSTNAMES = new Set(["maps.app.goo.gl"]);
const GOOGLE_MAPS_PLACE_IDENTIFIER_PARAMETERS = ["cid", "place_id", "query_place_id"];

export const SUPPORTED_REVIEW_PLATFORM = "google_maps" as const;

export type SupportedReviewPlatform = typeof SUPPORTED_REVIEW_PLATFORM;

export interface ValidatedAnalysisTargetInput {
  name: string;
  sourceUrl: string;
  platform: SupportedReviewPlatform;
}

export type AnalysisTargetInputField = "name" | "sourceUrl";

export type AnalysisTargetValidationErrorCode =
  | "INVALID_INPUT_TYPE"
  | "TARGET_NAME_REQUIRED"
  | "TARGET_NAME_INVALID_TYPE"
  | "TARGET_NAME_INVALID_CHARACTERS"
  | "TARGET_NAME_TOO_LONG"
  | "SOURCE_URL_REQUIRED"
  | "SOURCE_URL_INVALID_TYPE"
  | "SOURCE_URL_MALFORMED"
  | "SOURCE_URL_UNSUPPORTED_PROTOCOL"
  | "SOURCE_URL_TOO_LONG"
  | "SOURCE_URL_UNSUPPORTED_PLATFORM";

export interface AnalysisTargetValidationIssue {
  field?: AnalysisTargetInputField;
  code: AnalysisTargetValidationErrorCode;
  message: string;
}

export type AnalysisTargetValidationResult =
  | {
      success: true;
      data: ValidatedAnalysisTargetInput;
    }
  | {
      success: false;
      error: {
        code: "INVALID_ANALYSIS_TARGET_INPUT";
        message: "Please fix the highlighted fields.";
        issues: readonly AnalysisTargetValidationIssue[];
      };
    };

function hasOwnProperty(input: Record<string, unknown>, property: string): boolean {
  return Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(input, property);
}

function isRecord(input: unknown): input is Record<string, unknown> {
  return typeof input === "object" && input !== null && !Array.isArray(input);
}

function characterCount(value: string): number {
 * from the authenticated session.
 *
 * Shortened URLs such as maps.app.goo.gl are intentionally unsupported until S1-020 confirms that
 * the ingestion provider can use them without redirect resolution.
 */
export function validateAnalysisTargetInput(input: unknown): AnalysisTargetValidationResult {
  if (!isRecord(input)) {
    return failure([
      {
        code: "INVALID_INPUT_TYPE",
        message: "Target information must be an object.",
      },
    ]);
  }

  const issues: AnalysisTargetValidationIssue[] = [];
  const rawName = hasOwnProperty(input, "name") ? input.name : undefined;
  const rawSourceUrl = hasOwnProperty(input, "sourceUrl") ? input.sourceUrl : undefined;

  let normalizedName: string | undefined;
  let normalizedSourceUrl: string | undefined;

  if (rawName === undefined || rawName === null) {
    issues.push({
      field: "name",
      code: "TARGET_NAME_REQUIRED",
      message: TARGET_NAME_REQUIRED_MESSAGE,
    });
  } else if (typeof rawName !== "string") {
    issues.push({
      field: "name",
      code: "TARGET_NAME_INVALID_TYPE",
      message: TARGET_NAME_REQUIRED_MESSAGE,
    });
  } else {
    normalizedName = normalizeTargetName(rawName);

    if (normalizedName.length === 0) {
      issues.push({
        field: "name",
        code: "TARGET_NAME_REQUIRED",
        message: TARGET_NAME_REQUIRED_MESSAGE,
      });
    } else if (hasUnsafeControlCharacters(rawName)) {
      issues.push({
        field: "name",
        code: "TARGET_NAME_INVALID_CHARACTERS",
        message: TARGET_NAME_INVALID_CHARACTERS_MESSAGE,
      });
    } else if (characterCount(normalizedName) > MAX_TARGET_NAME_LENGTH) {
      issues.push({
        field: "name",
        code: "TARGET_NAME_TOO_LONG",
        message: TARGET_NAME_TOO_LONG_MESSAGE,
      });
    }
  }

  if (rawSourceUrl === undefined || rawSourceUrl === null) {
    issues.push({
      field: "sourceUrl",
      code: "SOURCE_URL_REQUIRED",
      message: SOURCE_URL_REQUIRED_MESSAGE,
    });
  } else if (typeof rawSourceUrl !== "string") {
    issues.push({
      field: "sourceUrl",
      code: "SOURCE_URL_INVALID_TYPE",
      message: SOURCE_URL_INVALID_MESSAGE,
    });
  } else {
    normalizedSourceUrl = rawSourceUrl.trim();

    if (normalizedSourceUrl.length === 0) {
      issues.push({
        field: "sourceUrl",
        code: "SOURCE_URL_REQUIRED",
        message: SOURCE_URL_REQUIRED_MESSAGE,
      });
    } else if (normalizedSourceUrl.length > MAX_SOURCE_URL_LENGTH) {
      issues.push({
        field: "sourceUrl",
        code: "SOURCE_URL_TOO_LONG",
        message: SOURCE_URL_TOO_LONG_MESSAGE,
      });
    } else {
      let parsedUrl: URL | undefined;

      try {
        parsedUrl = new URL(normalizedSourceUrl);
      } catch {
        issues.push({
          field: "sourceUrl",
          code: "SOURCE_URL_MALFORMED",
          message: SOURCE_URL_INVALID_MESSAGE,
        });
      }

      if (parsedUrl) {
        if (parsedUrl.protocol !== "http:" && parsedUrl.protocol !== "https:") {
          issues.push({
            field: "sourceUrl",
            code: "SOURCE_URL_UNSUPPORTED_PROTOCOL",
            message: SOURCE_URL_INVALID_MESSAGE,
          });
        } else if (!isGoogleMapsBusinessUrl(parsedUrl)) {
          issues.push({
            field: "sourceUrl",
            code: "SOURCE_URL_UNSUPPORTED_PLATFORM",
            message: unsupportedGoogleMapsMessage(parsedUrl),
          });
        } else {
          normalizedSourceUrl = parsedUrl.href;
        }
      }
    }
  }

  if (issues.length > 0 || normalizedName === undefined || normalizedSourceUrl === undefined) {
    return failure(issues);
  }

  return {
    success: true,
    data: {
      name: normalizedName,
      sourceUrl: normalizedSourceUrl,
      platform: SUPPORTED_REVIEW_PLATFORM,
    },
  };
}
=== Next route handlers doc
---
title: Route Handlers
nav_title: Route Handlers
description: Create custom request handlers with Next.js Route Handlers using the Web Request and Response APIs.
related:
  title: API Reference
  description: Learn more about Route Handlers
  links:
    - app/api-reference/file-conventions/route

## Route Handlers

Route Handlers allow you to create custom request handlers for a given route using the Web [Request](https://developer.mozilla.org/docs/Web/API/Request) and [Response](https://developer.mozilla.org/docs/Web/API/Response) APIs.

<Image
  alt="Route.js Special File"
  srcLight="/docs/light/route-special-file.png"
  srcDark="/docs/dark/route-special-file.png"
  width="1600"
  height="444"
/>

> **Good to know**: Route Handlers are only available inside the `app` directory. They are the equivalent of [API Routes](/docs/pages/building-your-application/routing/api-routes) inside the `pages` directory meaning you **do not** need to use API Routes and Route Handlers together.

### Convention

Route Handlers are defined in a [`route.js|ts` file](/docs/app/api-reference/file-conventions/route) inside the `app` directory:

```ts filename="app/api/route.ts" switcher
export async function GET(request: Request) {}
```

```js filename="app/api/route.js" switcher
export async function GET(request) {}
```

Route Handlers can be nested anywhere inside the `app` directory, similar to `page.js` and `layout.js`. But there **cannot** be a `route.js` file at the same route segment level as `page.js`.

### Supported HTTP Methods

The following [HTTP methods](https://developer.mozilla.org/docs/Web/HTTP/Methods) are supported: `GET`, `POST`, `PUT`, `PATCH`, `DELETE`, `HEAD`, and `OPTIONS`. If an unsupported method is called, Next.js will return a `405 Method Not Allowed` response.

### Extended `NextRequest` and `NextResponse` APIs

In addition to supporting the native [Request](https://developer.mozilla.org/docs/Web/API/Request) and [Response](https://developer.mozilla.org/docs/Web/API/Response) APIs, Next.js extends them with [`NextRequest`](/docs/app/api-reference/functions/next-request) and [`NextResponse`](/docs/app/api-reference/functions/next-response) to provide convenient helpers for advanced use cases.

### Caching

Route Handlers are not cached by default. You can, however, opt into caching for `GET` methods. Other supported HTTP methods are **not** cached. To cache a `GET` method, use a [route config option](/docs/app/guides/caching-without-cache-components#dynamic) such as `export const dynamic = 'force-static'` in your Route Handler file.

```ts filename="app/items/route.ts" switcher
export const dynamic = 'force-static'

export async function GET() {
  const res = await fetch('https://data.mongodb-api.com/...', {
    headers: {
      'Content-Type': 'application/json',
      'API-Key': process.env.DATA_API_KEY,
````

````text
## 6. Error-Handling Conventions

- Validate external input at an authoritative server boundary even when the browser also validates it.
- Handle expected errors explicitly and translate them into safe, understandable responses.
- Do not expose stack traces, SQL details, internal file paths, secrets, credentials, or provider responses containing sensitive information.
- Unexpected errors must be logged safely on the server and returned to the user as a generic error.
- Existing valid review data must not be replaced by fabricated or placeholder data when ingestion fails.

Standard HTTP status usage:

- `200` - successful request
- `201` - resource created
- `400` - invalid request or validation failure
- `401` - authentication required or invalid session
- `403` - authenticated user is not permitted to perform the operation
- `404` - requested resource is unavailable or cannot be disclosed
- `409` - conflict, such as an operation that violates a uniqueness rule
- `500` - unexpected server failure

The team must apply a consistent `403`/`404` policy to user-owned resources so responses do not reveal another user's private resource. The selected policy must be tested.

## 7. API and Service Response Conventions

Successful Route Handler response:

```json
{
  "success": true,
  "data": {}
}
```

Error response:

```json
{
  "success": false,
  "error": {
    "code": "INVALID_SOURCE_URL",
    "message": "The submitted source URL is invalid."
  }
}
```

Rules:

- Error `code` values are stable machine-readable identifiers; `message` values are safe user-readable explanations.
- Validation responses may include safe field-level details when useful.
- Route Handlers must use the shared response convention instead of inventing a new shape per endpoint.
- Service functions return typed results or throw documented application errors; they do not return ad hoc response objects.
- Protected endpoints derive identity from the verified Supabase session, never from a client-supplied owner ID.

## 8. Authentication, Authorization, and Ownership

- Every AnalysisTarget belongs to exactly one authenticated user.
- Review and IngestionAttempt access is authorized through the owning AnalysisTarget.
- Protected Route Handlers must verify the session, derive authenticated identity, verify resource ownership, and only then perform the operation.
- The frontend may improve navigation by hiding unavailable actions, but frontend filtering is never an authorization control.
- Client-supplied `userId`, `ownerId`, or equivalent values are not trusted for ownership decisions.
- Supabase RLS provides defense in depth but does not replace authorization inside protected server operations.
- Cross-user tests must use at least two identities and prove that User B cannot read, modify, delete, or ingest into User A's resources.
=== proxy

import { updateSession } from "@/lib/supabase/proxy";

export async function proxy(request: NextRequest) {
  return updateSession(request);
}

export const config = {
  matcher: [
    "/login",
    "/auth/:path*",
    "/api/auth/:path*",
    "/analysis-targets/:path*",
    "/api/analysis-targets/:path*",
  ],
};

=== S1-022
S1-013 **Outcome:**
An authenticated user cannot read, create, modify, or initiate ingestion against review data belonging to another user's AnalysisTarget.

**Technical Guidance:**

Review ownership may be inherited through the AnalysisTarget rather than duplicated on every Review record, but authorization must still be provable.

At minimum, automated tests should demonstrate that User B cannot:

1. Retrieve User A's target reviews.
2. Submit ingestion against User A's target.
3. Create review records under User A's target through a protected backend request.

The backend should validate ownership of the parent AnalysisTarget before performing child-resource operations.

**Rules:** S1-BR-007 thro

S1-022 **Outcome:**
Successfully normalized Reviews are persisted and associated with the correct AnalysisTarget.

**Technical Guidance:**

The persistence model must support later retrieval of all Reviews belonging to a selected AnalysisTarget.

The system must preserve the ownership boundary transitively through the target.

At minimum, tests should verify:

1. Reviews are persisted after successful ingestion.
2. Persisted review text and rating match normalized input.
3. Reviews are associated with the intended AnalysisTarget.
4. Reviews from Target A do not appear when retrieving Target B.
5. A user cannot retrieve persisted Reviews through another user's target.

Teams should consider how dupl

## 6. Retaining AI session transcripts

The team keeps the complete available transcript of every AI session that produced or shaped committed code. A summary does not replace the original conversation.

- **Where:** `ai-transcripts/` at the root of the repository.
- **Naming:** `YYYY-MM-DD_<author>_<issue-id>_<tool>.md` (for example `2026-11-02_shahdp2_S1-007_claude.md`).
- **What to save:** the original prompts and AI responses, including failed approaches, errors, corrections, and dead ends, plus a short note on what was accepted, changed, or rejected.
- **Redact first:** remove secrets, credentials, and personal data before committing a transcript.
- **Assume visibility:** transcripts are committed to the repository, so assume they may be seen by the whole team and the instructor.
````

**Tool: Bash** — Add the POST route and its unit tests, then run them

IN

````text
cd "[REDACTED LOCAL REPOSITORY PATH]" && mkdir -p src/app/api/analysis-targets tests/unit/app/api/analysis-targets
cat > src/app/api/analysis-targets/route.ts <<'EOF'
import { NextResponse } from "next/server";
import { validateAnalysisTargetInput } from "@/lib/validation/analysis-target";
import { createSupabaseServerClient } from "@/lib/supabase/server";
import {
  AnalysisTargetPersistenceError,
  createAnalysisTarget,
} from "@/services/analysis-targets/analysis-target-repository";
import { requireApiUser } from "@/services/auth/access-control";

const PRIVATE_HEADERS = { "Cache-Control": "private, no-store" };

export async function POST(request: Request) {
  const auth = await requireApiUser();
  if (auth.response) return auth.response;

  // Malformed JSON falls through to the validator, which rejects anything that isn't an object.
  const body: unknown = await request.json().catch(() => undefined);
  const input = validateAnalysisTargetInput(body);
  if (!input.success) {
    return NextResponse.json(
      { success: false, error: input.error },
      { status: 400, headers: PRIVATE_HEADERS },
    );
  }

  try {
    // Only validated fields reach persistence, and the owner is always the verified session user.
    // Any ownerId or userId in the body is ignored. The session-scoped client lets RLS re-check it.
    const client = await createSupabaseServerClient(true);
    const target = await createAnalysisTarget(client, auth.user.id, input.data);
    return NextResponse.json(
      { success: true, data: target },
      { status: 201, headers: PRIVATE_HEADERS },
    );
  } catch (error) {
    // Deliberately omit database errors, request input, and user identifiers.
    console.error("Analysis target operation failed", {
      operation: "create",
      category:
        error instanceof AnalysisTargetPersistenceError ? error.code : "UNEXPECTED_ERROR",
    });
    return NextResponse.json(
      {
        success: false,
        error: {
          code: "ANALYSIS_TARGET_CREATION_FAILED",
          message: "We couldn't create this target. Please try again.",
        },
      },
      { status: 500, headers: PRIVATE_HEADERS },
    );
  }
}
EOF
cat > tests/unit/app/api/analysis-targets/route.test.ts <<'EOF'
/** @jest-environment node */
import { POST } from "@/app/api/analysis-targets/route";
import { createSupabaseServerClient } from "@/lib/supabase/server";
import { getAuthenticatedUser } from "@/services/auth/auth-service";

jest.mock("@/services/auth/auth-service", () => ({ getAuthenticatedUser: jest.fn() }));
jest.mock("@/lib/supabase/server", () => ({ createSupabaseServerClient: jest.fn() }));
jest.mock("next/server", () => ({
  ...jest.requireActual("next/server"),
  connection: jest.fn().mockResolvedValue(undefined),
}));

const USER_A = { id: "11111111-1111-4111-8111-111111111111", email: "a@example.com" };
const USER_B = { id: "22222222-2222-4222-8222-222222222222", email: "b@example.com" };
const SOURCE_URL = "https://www.google.com/maps/place/Joe's+Pizza";
const validBody = { name: "Joe's Pizza", sourceUrl: SOURCE_URL };

// The real guard and repository run; only the database client is fake, so these tests assert the
// exact row the handler sends to Supabase.
function useDatabase(result?: { data: unknown; error: unknown }) {
  const insert = jest.fn((row: Record<string, unknown>) => ({
    select: () => ({
      single: async () =>
        result ?? {
          data: { id: "33333333-3333-4333-8333-333333333333", created_at: "2026-11-04", ...row },
          error: null,
        },
    }),
  }));
  const from = jest.fn(() => ({ insert }));
  jest.mocked(createSupabaseServerClient).mockResolvedValue({ from } as never);
  return { from, insert };
}

function post(body: unknown) {
  return POST(
    new Request("http://localhost/api/analysis-targets", {
      method: "POST",
      body: typeof body === "string" ? body : JSON.stringify(body),
    }),
  );
}

beforeEach(() => {
  jest.clearAllMocks();
  jest.spyOn(console, "error").mockImplementation(() => {});
});

describe("ownership", () => {
  it("persists the verified user as owner and returns the created target", async () => {
    jest.mocked(getAuthenticatedUser).mockResolvedValue(USER_A);
    const { from, insert } = useDatabase();

    const response = await post(validBody);

    expect(response.status).toBe(201);
    expect(response.headers.get("Cache-Control")).toContain("no-store");
    expect(from).toHaveBeenCalledWith("analysis_targets");
    expect(insert).toHaveBeenCalledWith({
      owner_id: USER_A.id,
      name: "Joe's Pizza",
      platform: "google_maps",
      source_url: SOURCE_URL,
      google_place_id: null,
    });
    await expect(response.json()).resolves.toMatchObject({
      success: true,
      data: { ownerId: USER_A.id, name: "Joe's Pizza", platform: "google_maps" },
    });
  });

  it.each(["ownerId", "owner_id", "userId", "user_id"])(
    "ignores another user's ID submitted as %s",
    async (field) => {
      jest.mocked(getAuthenticatedUser).mockResolvedValue(USER_A);
      const { insert } = useDatabase();

      const response = await post({ ...validBody, [field]: USER_B.id });

      expect(response.status).toBe(201);
      expect(insert.mock.calls[0][0].owner_id).toBe(USER_A.id);
      expect(JSON.stringify(insert.mock.calls)).not.toContain(USER_B.id);
    },
  );

  it("does not let submitted fields set database columns", async () => {
    jest.mocked(getAuthenticatedUser).mockResolvedValue(USER_A);
    const { insert } = useDatabase();

    await post({ ...validBody, id: "attacker-id", created_at: "2000-01-01", platform: "other" });

    expect(insert.mock.calls[0][0]).toEqual({
      owner_id: USER_A.id,
      name: "Joe's Pizza",
      platform: "google_maps",
      source_url: SOURCE_URL,
      google_place_id: null,
    });
  });

  it("uses each request's own verified identity for an identical body", async () => {
    const { insert } = useDatabase();

    jest.mocked(getAuthenticatedUser).mockResolvedValue(USER_A);
    await post(validBody);
    jest.mocked(getAuthenticatedUser).mockResolvedValue(USER_B);
    await post(validBody);

    expect(insert.mock.calls.map(([row]) => row.owner_id)).toEqual([USER_A.id, USER_B.id]);
  });

  it("uses a session-scoped client so Row Level Security also checks the owner", async () => {
    jest.mocked(getAuthenticatedUser).mockResolvedValue(USER_A);
    useDatabase();

    await post(validBody);

    expect(createSupabaseServerClient).toHaveBeenCalledWith(true);
  });
});

describe("authentication", () => {
  it("rejects a missing session before any database work, even with a submitted owner", async () => {
    jest.mocked(getAuthenticatedUser).mockResolvedValue(null);
    const { insert } = useDatabase();

    const response = await post({ ...validBody, ownerId: USER_A.id });

    expect(response.status).toBe(401);
    await expect(response.json()).resolves.toEqual({
      success: false,
      error: { code: "AUTH_REQUIRED", message: "Please sign in to continue." },
    });
    expect(createSupabaseServerClient).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
    expect(insert).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
  });

  it("fails closed when identity cannot be verified", async () => {
    jest.mocked(getAuthenticatedUser).mockRejectedValue(new Error("provider-secret-details"));
    const { insert } = useDatabase();

    const response = await post(validBody);

    expect(response.status).toBe(503);
    expect(await response.text()).not.toContain("provider-secret-details");
    expect(insert).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
  });
});

describe("validation", () => {
  beforeEach(() => {
    jest.mocked(getAuthenticatedUser).mockResolvedValue(USER_A);
  });

  it("returns field issues and writes nothing for invalid input", async () => {
    const { insert } = useDatabase();

    const response = await post({ name: " ", sourceUrl: "https://example.com" });

    expect(response.status).toBe(400);
    expect(response.headers.get("Cache-Control")).toContain("no-store");
    await expect(response.json()).resolves.toMatchObject({
      success: false,
      error: {
        code: "INVALID_ANALYSIS_TARGET_INPUT",
        issues: [
          { field: "name", code: "TARGET_NAME_REQUIRED" },
          { field: "sourceUrl", code: "SOURCE_URL_UNSUPPORTED_PLATFORM" },
        ],
      },
    });
    expect(insert).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
  });

  it("treats malformed JSON as invalid input", async () => {
    const { insert } = useDatabase();

    const response = await post("{not json");

    expect(response.status).toBe(400);
    await expect(response.json()).resolves.toMatchObject({
      error: { code: "INVALID_ANALYSIS_TARGET_INPUT" },
    });
    expect(insert).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
  });
});

describe("persistence failures", () => {
  beforeEach(() => {
    jest.mocked(getAuthenticatedUser).mockResolvedValue(USER_A);
  });

  it("returns a generic error and logs no database details when the write is denied", async () => {
    useDatabase({ data: null, error: { message: "new row violates row-level security policy" } });

    const response = await post(validBody);

    expect(response.status).toBe(500);
    const body = await response.json();
    expect(body).toEqual({
      success: false,
      error: {
        code: "ANALYSIS_TARGET_CREATION_FAILED",
        message: "We couldn't create this target. Please try again.",
      },
    });
    expect(console.error).toHaveBeenCalledWith("Analysis target operation failed", {
      operation: "create",
      category: "ANALYSIS_TARGET_PERSISTENCE_FAILED",
    });
    expect(JSON.stringify(jest.mocked(console.error).mock.calls)).not.toContain("row-level");
  });

  it("returns the same generic error when the database client cannot be created", async () => {
    jest.mocked(createSupabaseServerClient).mockRejectedValue(new Error("missing configuration"));

    const response = await post(validBody);

    expect(response.status).toBe(500);
    expect(await response.text()).not.toContain("missing configuration");
    expect(console.error).toHaveBeenCalledWith("Analysis target operation failed", {
      operation: "create",
      category: "UNEXPECTED_ERROR",
    });
  });
});
EOF
````

````text
cat > docs/s1-011-ownership.md <<'EOF'
# S1-011: Ownership from authenticated identity

## Decision

ReviewLens stores the Supabase Auth user ID (`auth.users.id`) as the owner of user-owned records.
This is the "provider user identifier stored as owner" approach from the story. There is no separate
internal User table, because nothing in Sprint 1 needs profile data that Supabase Auth doesn't hold.

Ownership is enforced at two independent layers:

1. **Application:** the Route Handler takes the owner only from `requireApiUser()`, which verifies
   the session with Supabase Auth on each request.
2. **Database:** Row Level Security requires `auth.uid() = owner_id`, using a client that carries the
   user's session. A service-role client bypasses this and must not be used for user requests.

## Rules for user-owned records

- Never read an owner from the request. `ownerId`, `userId`, and similar body fields are ignored, not
  rejected, so a spoofed value has no effect.
- Pass only validated fields to persistence. Repository inputs don't include ownership, so it remains
  a separate argument supplied by the server.
- Authenticate before reading the request body or creating a database client.
- `AnalysisTarget` stores `owner_id` directly. Reviews and ingestion attempts inherit ownership through
  their parent target, as the engineering standards (section 8) describe. S1-013 and S1-022 own that
  parent check and its tests.

## Implemented endpoint

`POST /api/analysis-targets` accepts `{ name, sourceUrl }` and matches the S1-015 client contract:

| Status | When                                      | `error.code`                      |
| ------ | ----------------------------------------- | --------------------------------- |
| 201    | Target committed; `data` is the target    | —                                 |
| 400    | Invalid or malformed input; nothing saved | `INVALID_ANALYSIS_TARGET_INPUT`   |
| 401    | No valid session; nothing saved           | `AUTH_REQUIRED`                   |
| 503    | Identity could not be verified            | `AUTH_UNAVAILABLE`                |
| 500    | Unexpected or persistence failure         | `ANALYSIS_TARGET_CREATION_FAILED` |

## Verification

- `tests/unit/app/api/analysis-targets/route.test.ts` runs the real guard, validator, and repository
  with a fake database client and asserts the exact row sent to Supabase.
- `npm run test:db` proves at PostgreSQL that RLS rejects a row whose owner isn't the current user.

These don't exercise the handler against a live Supabase project. That check needs the
`analysis_targets` migration applied to the team project and two signed-in test accounts: create a
target as each user with a spoofed `ownerId`, then confirm each row's `owner_id` is the signed-in user.
EOF
````

````text
import { defineConfig, devices } from "@playwright/test";

// Deterministic integration test: the real app + SDK talk only to a local fixture.
export default defineConfig({
  testDir: "./tests/e2e/auth",
  workers: 1,
  reporter: "list",
  use: {
    baseURL: "http://127.0.0.1:3100",
    ...devices["Desktop Chrome"],
    channel: process.env.PLAYWRIGHT_CHANNEL || undefined,
  },
  webServer: [
    {
      command: "node tests/fixtures/auth-provider.mjs",
      url: "http://127.0.0.1:54329/ready",
      reuseExistingServer: false,
    },
    {
      command: "node node_modules/next/dist/bin/next dev --hostname 127.0.0.1 --port 3100",
      url: "http://127.0.0.1:3100",
      reuseExistingServer: false,
      timeout: 120_000,
      env: {
        NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL: "http://127.0.0.1:54329",
        NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY: "dummy-publishable-key",
        NEXT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED: "1",
      },
    },
  ],
});
// Local test double only. Never deploy or use as an authentication implementation.
import { createServer } from "node:http";
import { createHmac } from "node:crypto";

const user = {
  id: "00000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000009",
  email: "fixture@example.com",
  aud: "authenticated",
  role: "authenticated",
  app_metadata: { provider: "email", providers: ["email"] },
  user_metadata: {},
  identities: [],
  created_at: "2026-01-01T00:00:00Z",
};
const tokens = new Map();
const refreshTokens = new Set();
let refreshed = 0;
let sequence = 0;

function session(lifetime = 3600) {
  const now = Math.floor(Date.now() / 1000);
  const header = Buffer.from(JSON.stringify({ alg: "HS256", typ: "JWT" })).toString("base64url");
  const payload = Buffer.from(
    JSON.stringify({
      sub: user.id,
      email: user.email,
      aud: "authenticated",
      role: "authenticated",
      iat: now,
      exp: now + lifetime,
      jti: String(++sequence),
    }),
  ).toString("base64url");
  const signature = createHmac("sha256", "dummy-test-signing-secret")
    .update(`${header}.${payload}`)
    .digest("base64url");
  const token = `${header}.${payload}.${signature}`;
  const refresh = `dummy-refresh-${sequence}`;
  tokens.set(token, now + lifetime);
  refreshTokens.add(refresh);
  return {
    access_token: token,
    refresh_token: refresh,
    token_type: "bearer",
    expires_in: lifetime,
    expires_at: now + lifetime,
    user,
  };
}

createServer(async (request, response) => {
  const url = new URL(request.url, "http://127.0.0.1:54329");
  response.setHeader("Content-Type", "application/json");
  response.setHeader("Cache-Control", "no-store");
  const send = (status, body) => {
    response.writeHead(status);
    response.end(JSON.stringify(body));
  };
  if (url.pathname === "/ready") return send(200, { ready: true });
  if (url.pathname === "/stats") return send(200, { refreshed });
  // Explicit test control: inject an expired session after login to exercise Proxy.
  if (url.pathname === "/expired-session") return send(200, session(-60));

  const chunks = [];
  for await (const chunk of request) chunks.push(chunk);
  let body;
  try {
    body = chunks.length ? JSON.parse(Buffer.concat(chunks).toString()) : {};
  } catch {
    return send(400, { code: "bad_json", message: "Invalid test request" });
  }

  const token = (request.headers.authorization || "").replace(/^Bearer /i, "");
  if (url.pathname === "/auth/v1/token") {
    if (url.searchParams.get("grant_type") === "refresh_token") {
      if (!refreshTokens.delete(body.refresh_token))
        return send(400, { code: "refresh_token_not_found", message: "Invalid fixture refresh" });
      refreshed++;
      return send(200, session());
    }
    if (body.email === user.email && body.password === "dummy-password")
      return send(200, session());
    return send(400, { code: "invalid_credentials", message: "Fixture credentials rejected" });
  }
  if (url.pathname === "/auth/v1/user") {
    const expiry = tokens.get(token);
    return expiry && expiry > Math.floor(Date.now() / 1000)
      ? send(200, user)
      : send(401, { code: "bad_jwt", message: "Fixture token rejected" });
  }
  if (url.pathname === "/auth/v1/logout") {
    tokens.delete(token);
    refreshTokens.clear();
    return send(200, {});
  }
  if (url.pathname === "/auth/v1/signup") return send(200, { ...user, email: body.email });
  return send(404, { message: "Unsupported fixture endpoint" });
}).listen(54329, "127.0.0.1");
import { expect, test } from "@playwright/test";

test("establishes, refreshes, preserves, and clears SDK cookie sessions", async ({
  page,
  context,
  request,
}) => {
  await page.goto("/login");
  const form = page.getByRole("form", { name: "Sign in" });
  await expect(form).toBeVisible();
  await page.getByLabel("Email *").fill("fixture@example.com");
  await page.getByLabel("Password *").fill("incorrect-password");
  await form.getByRole("button", { name: "Sign in", exact: true }).click();
  await expect(form.getByRole("alert")).toContainText("We couldn't sign you in");
  await expect(page.getByLabel("Email *")).toHaveValue("fixture@example.com");

  await page.getByLabel("Password *").fill("dummy-password");
  await form.getByRole("button", { name: "Sign in", exact: true }).click();
  await expect(page.getByRole("status")).toContainText("fixture@example.com");

  // Use only dummy fixture data to expire cookie storage deterministically.
  // Normal login tokens retain Supabase's recommended lifetime rather than
  // expiring during the Server Action's own render.
  const authCookies = (await context.cookies()).filter((cookie) =>
    cookie.name.includes("auth-token"),
  );
  expect(authCookies).toHaveLength(1);
  const expiredSession = await (await request.get("http://127.0.0.1:54329/expired-session")).json();
  await context.addCookies([
    {
      ...authCookies[0],
      value: `base64-${Buffer.from(JSON.stringify(expiredSession)).toString("base64url")}`,
    },
  ]);
  await page.reload();
  await expect(page.getByRole("status")).toContainText("fixture@example.com");
  await expect
    .poll(async () => (await (await request.get("http://127.0.0.1:54329/stats")).json()).refreshed)
    .toBeGreaterThan(0);

  await page.reload();
  await expect(page.getByRole("status")).toContainText("fixture@example.com");
  await page.getByRole("link", { name: "Back to home" }).click();
  await page.getByRole("link", { name: "Sign in or register" }).click();
  await expect(page.getByRole("status")).toContainText("fixture@example.com");
````

````text
cat > $SP/fake-supabase.mjs <<'EOF'
import { createServer } from "node:http";
const users = { a: "11111111-1111-4111-8111-111111111111", b: "22222222-2222-4222-8222-222222222222" };
const jwt = (sub) => {
  const e = (o) => Buffer.from(JSON.stringify(o)).toString("base64url");
  const now = Math.floor(Date.now()/1000);
  return `${e({alg:"HS256",typ:"JWT"})}.${e({sub,aud:"authenticated",role:"authenticated",iat:now,exp:now+3600,email:sub+"@example.com"})}.sig`;
};
const sub = (t) => { try { return JSON.parse(Buffer.from(t.split(".")[1],"base64url")).sub } catch { return null } };
const inserts = [];
createServer(async (req, res) => {
  const url = new URL(req.url, "http://x");
  const send = (s, b) => { res.writeHead(s, {"Content-Type":"application/json"}); res.end(JSON.stringify(b)); };
  const chunks = []; for await (const c of req) chunks.push(c);
  const token = (req.headers.authorization||"").replace(/^Bearer /i,"");
  if (url.pathname === "/ready") return send(200,{});
  if (url.pathname === "/inserts") return send(200,inserts);
  if (url.pathname.startsWith("/session/")) {
    const id = users[url.pathname.split("/")[2]]; const now=Math.floor(Date.now()/1000);
    return send(200,{access_token:jwt(id),refresh_token:"r-"+id,token_type:"bearer",expires_in:3600,expires_at:now+3600,user:{id,aud:"authenticated",email:id+"@example.com",app_metadata:{},user_metadata:{},created_at:"2026-01-01T00:00:00Z"}});
  }
  if (url.pathname === "/auth/v1/user") {
    const id = sub(token);
    return Object.values(users).includes(id) ? send(200,{id,aud:"authenticated",role:"authenticated",email:id+"@example.com",app_metadata:{},user_metadata:{},created_at:"2026-01-01T00:00:00Z"}) : send(401,{code:"bad_jwt",message:"rejected"});
  }
  if (url.pathname === "/rest/v1/analysis_targets" && req.method === "POST") {
    const row = JSON.parse(Buffer.concat(chunks).toString());
    inserts.push({ tokenSub: sub(token), row });
    return send(201,{id:"33333333-3333-4333-8333-333333333333",created_at:"2026-11-04T00:00:00Z",...row});
  }
  send(404,{message:"unsupported "+url.pathname});
}).listen(54330,"127.0.0.1");
EOF
cat > $SP/drive.mjs <<'EOF'
const API="http://127.0.0.1:3101/api/analysis-targets", DB="http://127.0.0.1:54330";
const url="https://www.google.com/maps/place/Joe's+Pizza";
const cookie=async(u)=>{const s=await (await fetch(`${DB}/session/${u}`)).json();return `sb-127-auth-token=base64-${Buffer.from(JSON.stringify(s)).toString("base64url")}`};
const post=async(body,c)=>{const r=await fetch(API,{method:"POST",redirect:"manual",headers:{"content-type":"application/json",...(c?{cookie:c}:{})},body:typeof body==="string"?body:JSON.stringify(body)});return {status:r.status,cache:r.headers.get("cache-control"),body:await r.text()}};
const A="11111111-1111-4111-8111-111111111111",B="22222222-2222-4222-8222-222222222222";
const show=(l,r)=>console.log(`${l}\n  -> ${r.status} [${r.cache}] ${r.body.slice(0,230)}`);
const inserts=async()=>(await fetch(`${DB}/inserts`)).json();
show("no cookie, spoofed ownerId=A",await post({name:"x",sourceUrl:url,ownerId:A}));
show("garbage cookie",await post({name:"x",sourceUrl:url},"sb-127-auth-token=garbage"));
console.log("inserts after signed-out attempts:",(await inserts()).length);
const ca=await cookie("a"),cb=await cookie("b");

show("User A, spoofed ownerId=B",await post({name:"From A",sourceUrl:url,ownerId:B,userId:B,owner_id:B},ca));
show("User B, spoofed ownerId=A",await post({name:"From B",sourceUrl:url,ownerId:A,userId:A,owner_id:A},cb));
const i=await inserts();console.log("database calls:",JSON.stringify(i,null,1));
const ok=i.length===2&&i[0].row.owner_id===A&&i[0].tokenSub===A&&i[1].row.owner_id===B&&i[1].tokenSub===B;

const g=await fetch(API);console.log("GET ->",g.status);
EOF
````

````text
The application can persist and retrieve an AnalysisTarget with its required Sprint 1 fields and authenticated owner relationship.
````

````text
EOF
````

### Claude

S1-011 is built and working locally, but nothing is committed or pushed yet, and it hasn't been tested against the real Supabase project. All checks pass: formatting, lint, typecheck, build, 223 unit tests (14 new) and the Postgres/RLS suite.

## What I built
- **Endpoint:** [route.ts](src/app/api/analysis-targets/route.ts) adds `POST /api/analysis-targets`, the handler S1-010's hand-off assigned to this story. The owner always comes from the verified session, so any `ownerId` or `userId` in the request is ignored.
- **Tests:** [route.test.ts](tests/unit/app/api/analysis-targets/route.test.ts) checks the exact row sent to the database.
- **Ownership decision:** [s1-011-ownership.md](docs/s1-011-ownership.md) records that we store the Supabase user ID as `owner_id`, with RLS as a second check. Both READMEs are updated too.
- **Transcript:** [2026-11-04_aryan_S1-011_claude.md](ai-transcripts/2026-11-04_aryan_S1-011_claude.md) is exported and redacted. Its accepted/changed review notes are marked pending for you to fill in, and I'll re-export it before we commit.

## How I tested the spoofing case
- **Protection check:** I temporarily made the handler trust a client-sent `ownerId`, and a test failed as it should.
- **Real app over HTTP:** I ran the real app and Supabase SDK against a throwaway local stand-in for Supabase:

| Request | Result |
|---|---|
| Signed out, or a bad cookie | 401, nothing saved |
| Invalid input or malformed JSON | 400, nothing saved |
| User A sends `ownerId` = B | 201, owner is A, sent with A's token |
| User B sends `ownerId` = A | 201, owner is B, sent with B's token |

## Still open
- **Live test:** the stand-in didn't enforce RLS, and the Postgres suite tests RLS separately from the app. To test it live, the `analysis_targets` migration has to be applied to the team's Supabase project, then you create a target from two accounts with a spoofed `ownerId`.
- **Merge commits:** I made two local merge commits on `feature/S1-011-owned-records`, pulling in S1-014 and S1-016 on top of S1-010. Teammates' branches are unchanged.
- **Unmerged dependencies:** #37 (S1-009) and #32 (S1-016) still have changes requested, and #39 (S1-010) is a draft, so this branch may need updating as they change. #35 (your S1-014) is approved and can merge.
- **Security review:** this PR needs a second, security-focused reviewer under `docs/AI-Assisted-Development.md` section 7.
- **Tell Neel:** S1-015's docs list this same handler as future work, so Neel should know it now exists to avoid building it twice. It follows their client contract, and the client should work with the 201 and 400 responses, though I didn't run their form against it.

Waiting for all three dependencies gives the cleanest diff, but teammates can't see or build on this in the meantime.
