## What this adds

`POST /api/analysis-targets`, which creates an AnalysisTarget owned by the verified session user. Any `ownerId`, `userId`, or similar field in the request is ignored.

- `src/app/api/analysis-targets/route.ts`: authenticates with `requireApiUser()`, validates with S1-016, then persists through the S1-014 repository using a session-scoped client.
- `docs/s1-011-ownership.md`: the ownership decision and rules for user-owned records.
- `tests/unit/app/api/analysis-targets/route.test.ts`: 14 tests using the real guard, validator, and repository with a fake database client.

Refs #11. This stays a draft, and the issue stays open, until the dependencies merge and live verification is done.

## Reviewing this stacked PR

The S1-011 changes are in commit 741056e, so **review that commit, not the full diff**.

This PR targets `feature/S1-010-protected-routes` (#39), which is itself based on S1-009 (#37). S1-014 (#35) and S1-016 (#32) aren't in that base, so their changes also appear in the full diff until they merge. The branch merges them locally; no teammate branch was changed. Once the dependencies merge, this branch needs updating and retargeting to `main`.

The S1-014 merge conflicted in `README.md`, `package.json`, and `package-lock.json`. Both sides were kept and the lockfile was regenerated. S1-015 (#36) is deliberately not included, because its lowercase `ui/` files collide with S1-009's PascalCase files on case-insensitive filesystems.

## Ownership decision

The Supabase Auth user ID (`auth.users.id`) is stored as `owner_id`, the story's "provider user identifier stored as owner" option. Ownership is enforced at two layers:

1. **Application:** the owner comes only from the verified session.
2. **Database:** the S1-014 RLS policy requires `auth.uid() = owner_id`, using a client that carries the user's session.

## API contract

Accepts `{ name, sourceUrl }`, as the S1-015 client sends it. Responses are `private, no-store`.

| Status | When | `error.code` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 201 | Target committed; `data` is the target | — |
| 400 | Invalid or malformed input; nothing saved | `INVALID_ANALYSIS_TARGET_INPUT` |
| 401 | No valid session; nothing saved | `AUTH_REQUIRED` |
| 503 | Identity could not be verified | `AUTH_UNAVAILABLE` |
| 500 | Unexpected or persistence failure | `ANALYSIS_TARGET_CREATION_FAILED` |

S1-015's docs list this handler as future work, so S1-015 can use it rather than adding its own. The form has not been run against it.

## Test evidence

Passed locally:

- `npm run format:check`, `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`
- `npm test`: 17 suites, 223 tests
- `npm run test:db` against local PostgreSQL 16

**Sabotage check:** making the handler trust a client-sent `ownerId` made the suite fail.

**Local HTTP check:** the real app and Supabase SDK were run against a throwaway stand-in with two users that recorded database calls. It is not committed.

| Request | Result |
| --- | --- |
| Signed out, or a bad cookie | 401, no database call |
| Invalid input or malformed JSON | 400, no database call |
| User A sends `ownerId` = B | 201, `owner_id` is A, sent with A's token |
| User B sends `ownerId` = A | 201, `owner_id` is B, sent with B's token |

## Not verified

The stand-in does not enforce RLS, and `npm run test:db` tests RLS separately from the app, so **the handler has not been run against real Supabase**. That needs the `analysis_targets` migration applied to the team project, then a target created from two accounts with a spoofed `ownerId`, confirming each row's `owner_id` is the signed-in user.

`npm run test:e2e:auth` was not run on this branch.

## Security notes (for the S1-004 §7 security review)

This PR writes user-owned data, so it needs the additional security-focused reviewer.

- The session is verified before the body is read or a database client is created.
- Only validated fields reach persistence, so a submitted `id`, `created_at`, or `platform` has no effect.
- No service-role key is used.
- Failures return a generic message. Logs contain only the operation and an error category, with no database details, request input, or user IDs.

=== Shah comment
shahdp2 2026-11-04T22:28:45Z
Looks good to me The ownership logic is solid:

owner_id comes only from the verified session (requireApiUser → getUser()). Spoofed ownerId/userId/owner_id/user_id in the body never reach the insert.
It uses the session-scoped client (no service role), so the S1-014 RLS auth.uid() = owner_id policy checks the owner a second time.
It authenticates before parsing the body or creating the DB client. Errors and logs leak no DB details or IDs, and responses are private, no-store.
The tests are thorough: real guard, validator and repo, exact inserted row checked, two-user case, protected columns can't be overwritten. The sabotage check is a nice touch.
The ownership decision doc covers the story's requirement to pick one approach and apply it to all user-scoped records.
Non-blocking notes:

As you mentioned, it still needs one live check against the team Supabase project (migration applied, two accounts, spoofed ownerId) before marking ready.
Once #39/#37/#35/#32 merge, retarget to main and re-run CI, since the full diff includes their changes for now.
Minor edge case: if the session expires between requireApiUser and the insert, RLS rejects it and the user gets a 500 instead of a 401. Fine for Sprint 1, just noting it.
Will Approve  the S1-011 changes once the live check is done. ✅

import type {
  SupportedReviewPlatform,
  ValidatedAnalysisTargetInput,
} from "@/lib/validation/analysis-target";

export interface AnalysisTarget {
  id: string;
  name: string;
  sourceUrl: string;
  platform: SupportedReviewPlatform;
}

export type CreateAnalysisTarget = (input: ValidatedAnalysisTargetInput) => Promise<AnalysisTarget>;
function isAnalysisTarget(value: unknown): value is AnalysisTarget {
  return (
    isRecord(value) &&
    typeof value.id === "string" &&
    value.id.length > 0 &&
    typeof value.name === "string" &&
    typeof value.sourceUrl === "string" &&
    value.platform === "google_maps"
  );
}

async function readJson(response: Response): Promise<unknown> {

**Tool: Bash** — Rerun e2e, then push, retarget PR to main, update body, await CI

IN

````text
SP=[REDACTED SCRATCHPAD PATH]
cd "[REDACTED LOCAL REPOSITORY PATH]"; ls -t [REDACTED LOCAL PATH] | head -1 | xargs basename | cut -c1-8
PLAYWRIGHT_CHANNEL=chrome npm run -s test:e2e:auth > $SP/e2e.txt 2>&1; RC=$?; echo "full e2e exit=$RC"; grep -E '^\s+[0-9]+ (passed|failed)' $SP/e2e.txt; rm -rf test-results; [ $RC = 0 ] || { echo STOP; exit 0; }
cat > $SP/pr.md <<'EOF'
## What this adds

`POST /api/analysis-targets`, which creates an AnalysisTarget owned by the verified session user. Any `ownerId`, `userId`, or similar field in the request is ignored.

- `src/app/api/analysis-targets/route.ts`: authenticates with `requireApiUser()`, validates with S1-016, then persists through the S1-014 repository using a session-scoped client.
- `docs/s1-011-ownership.md`: the ownership decision and rules for user-owned records.
- `tests/unit/app/api/analysis-targets/route.test.ts`: 14 tests using the real guard, validator, and repository with a fake database client.

Refs #11. This stays a draft, and the issue stays open, until the live verification below is done.

## Update: synced with `main`

S1-009, S1-010, S1-012, S1-014, and S1-016 have merged, so this PR now targets `main` and its diff contains only S1-011 files. Where the merge conflicted, `main`'s reviewed versions were kept.

The final S1-014 migration lets clients insert only `owner_id`, `name`, `platform`, and `source_url`, and its repository no longer sends `google_place_id`. The route tests were updated to match; the handler did not need changing.

## Ownership decision

The Supabase Auth user ID (`auth.users.id`) is stored as `owner_id`, the story's "provider user identifier stored as owner" option. Ownership is enforced at two layers:

1. **Application:** the owner comes only from the verified session.
2. **Database:** the S1-014 RLS policy requires `auth.uid() = owner_id`, using a client that carries the user's session.

## API contract

Accepts `{ name, sourceUrl }`. Responses are `private, no-store`. On success, `data` contains `id`, `ownerId`, `name`, `platform`, `sourceUrl`, `googlePlaceId`, and `createdAt`.

| Status | When | `error.code` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 201 | Target committed | — |
| 400 | Invalid or malformed input; nothing saved | `INVALID_ANALYSIS_TARGET_INPUT` |
| 401 | No valid session; nothing saved | `AUTH_REQUIRED` |
| 503 | Identity could not be verified | `AUTH_UNAVAILABLE` |
| 500 | Unexpected or persistence failure | `ANALYSIS_TARGET_CREATION_FAILED` |

## Test evidence

Passed locally on the merged code:

- `npm run format:check`, `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`
- `npm test`: 18 suites, 288 tests
- `npm run test:db` against local PostgreSQL 16
- `npm run test:e2e:auth`: 5 of 5, using installed Chrome (`PLAYWRIGHT_CHANNEL=chrome`). The first full run had one timeout on a cold dev server; that test then passed on repeat runs here and on clean `main`, as did a full rerun.

**Sabotage check:** before the sync, making the handler trust a client-sent `ownerId` made the suite fail.

**Local HTTP check:** the real app and Supabase SDK were run against a throwaway stand-in with two users that recorded database calls. It is not committed.

| Request | Result |
| --- | --- |
| Signed out, or a bad cookie | 401, no database call |
| Invalid input or malformed JSON | 400, no database call |
| User A sends `ownerId` = B | 201, `owner_id` is A, sent with A's token |
| User B sends `ownerId` = A | 201, `owner_id` is B, sent with B's token |

The 201 and 400 responses also satisfied the checks in S1-015's browser client at 9012ba3. The S1-015 form itself has not been run against this endpoint.

## Not verified

The stand-in does not enforce RLS, and `npm run test:db` tests RLS separately from the app, so **the handler has not been run against real Supabase**. It is also not confirmed that the migration has been applied to the shared project. Verification needs:

1. The final `supabase/migrations/20261103120000_create_analysis_targets.sql` from `main` applied to the team project. This file changed during S1-014 review, so a project with an earlier version applied won't match.
2. A target created from two accounts with a spoofed `ownerId`, confirming each row's `owner_id` is the signed-in user.

## Known limitation

If a session expires between `requireApiUser()` and the insert, RLS rejects the write and the user gets a 500 rather than a 401. Noted as non-blocking for Sprint 1 in review.

## Security notes (for the S1-004 §7 security review)

This PR writes user-owned data, so it needs the additional security-focused reviewer.

- The session is verified before the body is read or a database client is created.
- Only validated fields reach persistence, so a submitted `id`, `created_at`, or `platform` has no effect.
- No service-role key is used.
- Failures return a generic message. Logs contain only the operation and an error category, with no database details, request input, or user IDs.

## AI-assisted development

Claude Code was used for implementation and testing. Transcript: `ai-transcripts/2026-11-04_aryan_S1-011_claude.md`.
EOF
python3 $SP/export.py; F=ai-transcripts/2026-11-04_aryan_S1-011_claude.md; echo "leaks: $(grep -cE '[REDACTED USER]|aum2[3]|8193721[3]' "$F")"; wc -l "$F"; grep -c '^### Aryan' "$F"; git add "$F" && git commit -q -m "docs: update S1-011 transcript after syncing with main" ; git fetch -q origin main; git rev-list --count HEAD..origin/main; git push 2>&1 | tail -1; gh pr edit 40 --base main --body-file $SP/pr.md 2>&1 | tail -1
perl -e 'sleep 25'; for i in $(seq 1 24); do n=$(gh pr view 40 --json statusCheckRollup --jq '[.statusCheckRollup[]|select(.conclusion==null or .conclusion=="")]|length'); [ "$n" = 0 ] && break; perl -e 'sleep 15'; done
gh pr view 40 --json baseRefName,isDraft,mergeable,mergeStateStatus,changedFiles,statusCheckRollup --jq '"base=\(.baseRefName) draft=\(.isDraft) \(.mergeable)/\(.mergeStateStatus) files=\(.changedFiles)", (.statusCheckRollup[]|.name+": "+(.conclusion//.status))'; gh pr view 40 --json files --jq '.files[].path'; gh run list --branch feature/S1-011-owned-records --limit 1 --json conclusion,headSha --jq '.[]|[.conclusion,.headSha[0:7]]|@tsv'; git rev-parse --short HEAD; git status --short | wc -l
````

**Tool: Bash** — Check access and PR state, write and test read-only schema check

IN

````text
SP=[REDACTED SCRATCHPAD PATH]
cd "[REDACTED LOCAL REPOSITORY PATH]"; git status -sb | head -2; ls -a | grep -E '^\.env'; which supabase; gh pr view 40 --json isDraft,mergeable,mergeStateStatus,reviewDecision,reviews,comments --jq '"draft=\(.isDraft) \(.mergeable)/\(.mergeStateStatus) review=\(.reviewDecision)", (.reviews[]|.author.login+" "+.state), (.comments[]|.author.login+" "+.createdAt+": "+.body[0:200])'; git fetch -q origin main; echo "behind main: $(git rev-list --count HEAD..origin/main)"; git log HEAD..origin/main --format='%h %s' | cut -c1-90
cat > $SP/verify-deployed-schema.sql <<'EOF'
-- Read-only check that a Supabase project matches the final S1-014 migration.
-- Paste into the Supabase SQL editor. Every row should show pass = true.
with t as (select 'public.analysis_targets'::regclass as id),
checks(check_name, pass) as (
  values
  ('table has exactly the expected columns', (
    select array_agg(attname::text order by attname) = array['created_at','google_place_id','id','name','owner_id','platform','source_url']
    from pg_attribute, t where attrelid = t.id and attnum > 0 and not attisdropped)),
  ('RLS is enabled', (select relrowsecurity from pg_class, t where pg_class.oid = t.id)),
  ('policies are exactly select_own, insert_own, delete_own (no update)', (
    select array_agg(policyname::text order by policyname) = array['analysis_targets_delete_own','analysis_targets_insert_own','analysis_targets_select_own']
    from pg_policies where schemaname = 'public' and tablename = 'analysis_targets')),
  ('every policy applies to authenticated only', (
    select bool_and(roles = array['authenticated']::name[]) from pg_policies where schemaname = 'public' and tablename = 'analysis_targets')),
  ('select and delete policies check auth.uid() = owner_id', (
    select bool_and(qual like '%auth.uid()%' and qual like '%owner_id%') from pg_policies
    where schemaname = 'public' and tablename = 'analysis_targets' and cmd in ('SELECT','DELETE'))),
  ('insert policy checks auth.uid() = owner_id', (
    select bool_and(with_check like '%auth.uid()%' and with_check like '%owner_id%') from pg_policies
    where schemaname = 'public' and tablename = 'analysis_targets' and cmd = 'INSERT')),
  ('anon has no table access', (
    select not (has_table_privilege('anon', t.id, 'SELECT') or has_table_privilege('anon', t.id, 'INSERT')
             or has_table_privilege('anon', t.id, 'UPDATE') or has_table_privilege('anon', t.id, 'DELETE')) from t)),
  ('anon has no column access', (
    select not bool_or(has_column_privilege('anon', t.id, attname, 'SELECT,INSERT,UPDATE'))
    from pg_attribute, t where attrelid = t.id and attnum > 0 and not attisdropped)),
  ('authenticated can select and delete', (
    select has_table_privilege('authenticated', t.id, 'SELECT') and has_table_privilege('authenticated', t.id, 'DELETE') from t)),
  ('authenticated cannot update any column', (
    select not has_table_privilege('authenticated', t.id, 'UPDATE') from t)),
  ('authenticated cannot update individual columns', (
    select not bool_or(has_column_privilege('authenticated', t.id, attname, 'UPDATE'))
    from pg_attribute, t where attrelid = t.id and attnum > 0 and not attisdropped)),
  ('authenticated can insert only owner_id, name, platform, source_url', (
    select array_agg(attname::text order by attname) filter (where has_column_privilege('authenticated', t.id, attname, 'INSERT'))
           = array['name','owner_id','platform','source_url']
    from pg_attribute, t where attrelid = t.id and attnum > 0 and not attisdropped)),
  ('final Google Maps source URL constraint is present', (
    select count(*) = 1 from pg_constraint, t where conrelid = t.id and conname = 'analysis_targets_source_url_google_maps')),
  ('older source URL constraint is gone', (
    select count(*) = 0 from pg_constraint, t where conrelid = t.id and conname = 'analysis_targets_source_url_http')),
  ('name, platform, and place ID constraints are present', (
    select count(*) = 4 from pg_constraint, t where conrelid = t.id and conname in
      ('analysis_targets_name_present','analysis_targets_name_length','analysis_targets_platform_supported','analysis_targets_google_place_id_present'))),
  ('owner_id references auth.users and cascades on delete', (
    select count(*) = 1 from pg_constraint, t where conrelid = t.id and contype = 'f' and confrelid = 'auth.users'::regclass and confdeltype = 'c')),
  ('owner listing index is present', (to_regclass('public.analysis_targets_owner_id_created_at_idx') is not null))
)
select check_name, pass from checks order by pass, check_name;

### Claude

Neel's plan is sound, and the repo answers part of the question: **the PM set up the shared Supabase project**, but it never names them, so I don't want to guess. Your team will know who that is, and Shawn is the best person to ask first.

## What the repo shows
- **Who set it up:** `docs/s1-010-route-protection.md` says "The PM has created the shared Supabase project", and Shawn's S1-010 transcript says "The PM owns Supabase setup".
- **Who else has been in contact:** S1-009's PR says the PM confirmed the JWT expiry setting. Shawn's docs also report sign-in tests against the live project, so Shawn has the project's URL and key and has dealt with the PM directly.
- **Whether it's been applied:** like Neel, I found no record of the migration being deployed. Closed issue #14 doesn't settle it, and the S1-014 review only says it still had to be done.
- **GitHub access doesn't help:** repo collaborators are a separate list from Supabase dashboard access, so that list doesn't show who has dashboard access.

## Checking the migration history
Neel's order is right: inspect first, change nothing yet. Whoever has dashboard access can run this read-only query first:

```sql
select to_regclass('public.analysis_targets') as target_table,
       to_regclass('supabase_migrations.schema_migrations') as migration_history;
```

- **If `migration_history` isn't empty:** also run this query:

```sql
select version, name from supabase_migrations.schema_migrations order by version;
```

- **If `target_table` is empty:** nothing was applied, so the final migration from `main` can go on as is.
- **If `target_table` has a value:** run the 17-point schema check from my last message, which is the real test. An empty or missing history doesn't prove anything, because SQL pasted into the editor leaves no record.

## If an older version is deployed
Neel is right that we shouldn't reapply the original SQL, because it would simply fail on a table that already exists. I'd write a new corrective migration, which goes through normal review. It would first check for existing rows that break the stricter URL rule, and I'd test it locally against both the old and final schemas.

## One correction to Neel's message
The security review can't be done by you or me. Your team's rules require a second reviewer, so you need to ask a teammate to do it.

Written for: Neel, to paste into your team chat. I wrote it as though you don't know who the PM's Supabase contact is, so change that part if you do.

2. **If `target_table` has a value:** run the schema check, then send me the results:

   `[REDACTED SCRATCHPAD PATH]`
3. **If `target_table` is empty:** nothing was applied. Run `supabase/migrations/20261103120000_create_analysis_targets.sql` from `main` in the SQL editor, then run the schema check; all 17 rows should be `true`.
4. **Set up `.env.local`:** copy `.env.example` and add the project URL and publishable key from the dashboard. Don't use the service-role key.
5. **Run the two-user test:** follow the steps from my earlier message. If you'd rather I run it, give me two test accounts once `.env.local` is in place.

If any schema check rows are `false`, send them to me before changing anything, and I'll write the corrective migration.

### Claude

Neel's list is useful because it goes beyond the test I gave you: it also asks for signed-out creation and cross-user access on the live project, and my steps didn't cover those. Doing all of that by hand in browser consoles would be slow and easy to get wrong, so I'll write a script that runs every live check and prints results with IDs masked, ready to paste into #40. **Tool: Bash** — Read auth shim, Node version, env names and PR state

IN

````text
cd "[REDACTED LOCAL REPOSITORY PATH]"; cat tests/db/supabase-auth-shim.sql; node -v; cat .nvmrc; grep -n '"main"\|"module"\|"type"' node_modules/@supabase/ssr/package.json; sed -n 1,20p .env.example | grep -E '^[A-Z_]+='; git status -sb | head -1; git fetch -q origin; echo "behind main: $(git rev-list --count HEAD..origin/main)"; gh pr view 40 --json reviews,reviewDecision,mergeStateStatus --jq '.reviewDecision,.mergeStateStatus,(.reviews[]|.author.login+" "+.state)'
````

OUT

````text
-- Test-only stand-in for the parts of Supabase that migrations depend on, so they can run
-- against a plain local PostgreSQL. Never apply this to a real Supabase project: Supabase
-- already provides the auth schema, auth.uid(), and these roles.

do $$
begin
  if not exists (select 1 from pg_roles where rolname = 'anon') then
    create role anon nologin;
  end if;
  if not exists (select 1 from pg_roles where rolname = 'authenticated') then
    create role authenticated nologin;
  end if;
end
$$;

grant anon, authenticated to current_user;

create schema auth;
create table auth.users (id uuid primary key);

-- Same contract as Supabase: the signed-in user's id from the request JWT, or null.
create function auth.uid() returns uuid
language sql stable
as $$
  select (nullif(current_setting('request.jwt.claims', true), '')::jsonb ->> 'sub')::uuid
$$;

grant usage on schema auth to anon, authenticated;
grant execute on function auth.uid() to anon, authenticated;

-- Supabase grants table access to these roles by default and relies on RLS to restrict rows.
alter default privileges in schema public grant all on tables to anon, authenticated;
v25.6.0
24
5:  "main": "dist/main/index.js",
6:  "module": "dist/module/index.js",
16:    "type": "git",
NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL=
NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY=
## feature/S1-011-owned-records...origin/feature/S1-011-owned-records
behind main: 0
REVIEW_REQUIRED
BLOCKED
````

**Tool: Bash** — Write live-verification script and run it on real local Postgres

IN

````text
SP=[REDACTED SCRATCHPAD PATH]
cat > $SP/verify-live.mjs <<'EOF'
// S1-011 live verification. Run from the repository root with `npm run dev` already running on this branch.
// Reads NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL, NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY, and S1011_USER_{A,B}_{EMAIL,PASSWORD}
// from .env.local or the environment. Creates a few clearly named rows and deletes them at the end.
// Output masks IDs and omits emails, tokens, keys, and the project URL so it can be pasted into a PR.
import { createRequire } from "node:module";
import { existsSync } from "node:fs";
import { execSync } from "node:child_process";

if (existsSync(".env.local")) process.loadEnvFile(".env.local");
const require = createRequire(`${process.cwd()}/`);
const { createServerClient } = require("@supabase/ssr");

const env = process.env;
const SUPABASE = env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL;
const KEY = env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY;
const APP = env.S1011_APP_URL ?? "http://localhost:3000";
const missing = ["NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL", "NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY", "S1011_USER_A_EMAIL", "S1011_USER_A_PASSWORD", "S1011_USER_B_EMAIL", "S1011_USER_B_PASSWORD"].filter((n) => !env[n]);
if (missing.length) { console.error(`Missing settings: ${missing.join(", ")}`); process.exit(2); }

const REST = `${SUPABASE}/rest/v1/analysis_targets`;
const PREFIX = `S1-011 live check ${Date.now()}`;
const SOURCE_URL = "https://www.google.com/maps/place/Joes+Pizza";
const masks = new Map();
const mask = (text) => { let s = String(text); for (const [v, m] of masks) s = s.split(v).join(m); return s.replace(/[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}/gi, "[id]"); };

async function signIn(label, email, password) {
  const jar = new Map();
  const client = createServerClient(SUPABASE, KEY, { cookies: {
    getAll: () => [...jar].map(([name, value]) => ({ name, value })),
    setAll: (list) => { for (const { name, value } of list) jar.set(name, value); },
  } });
  const { data, error } = await client.auth.signInWithPassword({ email, password });
  if (error || !data.session) { console.error(`Could not sign in User ${label}. Check its credentials and that its email is confirmed.`); process.exit(2); }
  await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 200));
  if (!jar.size) { console.error("Sign-in produced no session cookie."); process.exit(2); }
  masks.set(data.user.id, `<user-${label}>`);
  return { id: data.user.id, token: data.session.access_token, cookie: [...jar].map(([n, v]) => `${n}=${v}`).join("; ") };
}

const api = async (body, cookie) => {
  const res = await fetch(`${APP}/api/analysis-targets`, { method: "POST", redirect: "manual", headers: { "content-type": "application/json", ...(cookie ? { cookie } : {}) }, body: typeof body === "string" ? body : JSON.stringify(body) });
  const text = await res.text(); let json = null; try { json = JSON.parse(text); } catch {}
  return { status: res.status, text, json, cache: res.headers.get("cache-control") ?? "" };
};
const rest = async (method, query, token, body) => {
  const res = await fetch(`${REST}?${query}`, { method, headers: { apikey: KEY, Authorization: `Bearer ${token ?? KEY}`, Prefer: "return=representation", ...(body ? { "content-type": "application/json" } : {}) }, ...(body ? { body: JSON.stringify(body) } : {}) });
  const text = await res.text(); let json = null; try { json = JSON.parse(text); } catch {}
  return { status: res.status, json, rows: Array.isArray(json) ? json : [] };
};
const byName = (name) => `select=*&name=eq.${encodeURIComponent(name)}`;
const denied = (r) => r.status >= 400 || r.rows.length === 0;
const describe = (r) => `HTTP ${r.status}, ${r.rows.length} row(s)${r.json?.code ? `, code ${r.json.code}` : ""}`;

const results = [];
const check = (group, name, pass, actual) => results.push({ group, name, pass: !!pass, actual: mask(actual) });
const SENSITIVE = /row-level|policy|relation|column |syntax|postgres|pgrst|stack|\bat \/|secret|service_role|eyJ[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10}/i;

const A = await signIn("A", env.S1011_USER_A_EMAIL, env.S1011_USER_A_PASSWORD);
const B = await signIn("B", env.S1011_USER_B_EMAIL, env.S1011_USER_B_PASSWORD);
if (A.id === B.id) { console.error("User A and User B must be different accounts."); process.exit(2); }

try {
  // Unauthenticated requests
  const outName = `${PREFIX} signed out`;
  let r = await api({ name: outName, sourceUrl: SOURCE_URL, ownerId: A.id, userId: A.id }, undefined);
  check("Unauthenticated", "POST with no session and a spoofed owner returns 401", r.status === 401 && r.json?.error?.code === "AUTH_REQUIRED", `HTTP ${r.status}, ${r.json?.error?.code}`);
  check("Unauthenticated", "That request created no row", (await rest("GET", byName(outName), A.token)).rows.length === 0, "0 rows expected");
  r = await api({ name: `${PREFIX} bad cookie`, sourceUrl: SOURCE_URL }, "sb-x-auth-token=not-a-session");
  check("Unauthenticated", "POST with an invalid cookie returns 401", r.status === 401, `HTTP ${r.status}`);
  const anonRead = await rest("GET", "select=id&limit=1", null);
  check("Unauthenticated", "Direct anonymous read is denied", anonRead.status >= 400, describe(anonRead));
  const anonWrite = await rest("POST", "select=*", null, { owner_id: A.id, name: `${PREFIX} anon`, platform: "google_maps", source_url: SOURCE_URL });
  check("Unauthenticated", "Direct anonymous insert is denied", anonWrite.status >= 400, describe(anonWrite));

  // Validation and safe errors
  const badName = `${PREFIX} invalid`;
  r = await api({ name: badName, sourceUrl: "https://example.com" }, A.cookie);
  check("Validation and errors", "Invalid input returns 400 with field issues", r.status === 400 && Array.isArray(r.json?.error?.issues), `HTTP ${r.status}, ${r.json?.error?.code}`);
  check("Validation and errors", "Invalid input created no row", (await rest("GET", byName(badName), A.token)).rows.length === 0, "0 rows expected");
  check("Validation and errors", "Error responses expose no sensitive details", !SENSITIVE.test(r.text), "400 and 401 bodies scanned");
  check("Validation and errors", "Responses are private and not cached", r.cache.includes("private") && r.cache.includes("no-store"), r.cache);

  // Ownership from the session
  const nameA = `${PREFIX} from A`, nameB = `${PREFIX} from B`;
  const ra = await api({ name: nameA, sourceUrl: SOURCE_URL, ownerId: B.id, userId: B.id, owner_id: B.id, user_id: B.id }, A.cookie);
  const rb = await api({ name: nameB, sourceUrl: SOURCE_URL, ownerId: A.id, userId: A.id, owner_id: A.id, user_id: A.id }, B.cookie);
  check("Ownership", "User A's request with a spoofed owner succeeds", ra.status === 201, `HTTP ${ra.status}${ra.json?.error?.code ? `, ${ra.json.error.code}` : ""}`);
  check("Ownership", "User B's request with a spoofed owner succeeds", rb.status === 201, `HTTP ${rb.status}${rb.json?.error?.code ? `, ${rb.json.error.code}` : ""}`);
  const d = ra.json?.data;
  check("Ownership", "Response has id, name, sourceUrl, and platform", d && typeof d.id === "string" && d.name === nameA && typeof d.sourceUrl === "string" && d.platform === "google_maps", d ? Object.keys(d).join(", ") : "no data");
  const rowA = (await rest("GET", byName(nameA), A.token)).rows[0];
  const rowB = (await rest("GET", byName(nameB), B.token)).rows[0];
  check("Ownership", "Stored owner of A's row is User A, not the spoofed ID", rowA?.owner_id === A.id, rowA ? `owner_id = ${rowA.owner_id}` : "row not found");
  check("Ownership", "Stored owner of B's row is User B, not the spoofed ID", rowB?.owner_id === B.id, rowB ? `owner_id = ${rowB.owner_id}` : "row not found");

  // Cross-user access, using User B's real token against User A's row
  if (rowA) {
    const q = `select=*&id=eq.${rowA.id}`;
    masks.set(rowA.id, "<target-A>");
    const read = await rest("GET", q, B.token);
    check("Cross-user access", "User B cannot read User A's row by known ID", read.rows.length === 0, describe(read));
    const upd = await rest("PATCH", q, B.token, { name: `${PREFIX} hijacked` });
    check("Cross-user access", "User B cannot update User A's row", denied(upd), describe(upd));
    const del = await rest("DELETE", q, B.token);
    check("Cross-user access", "User B cannot delete User A's row", denied(del), describe(del));
    const after = (await rest("GET", q, A.token)).rows[0];
    check("Cross-user access", "User A's row is unchanged afterwards", after && JSON.stringify(after) === JSON.stringify(rowA), after ? "row identical" : "row missing");
  } else {
    check("Cross-user access", "User A's row was available to test", false, "row not found");
  }
  const forged = await rest("POST", "select=*", B.token, { owner_id: A.id, name: `${PREFIX} forged`, platform: "google_maps", source_url: SOURCE_URL });
  check("Cross-user access", "User B cannot insert a row owned by User A", forged.status >= 400, describe(forged));

  // Column permissions, using each user's own token
  if (rowA) {
    const q = `select=*&id=eq.${rowA.id}`;
    const own = await rest("PATCH", q, A.token, { source_url: `${SOURCE_URL}2` });
    check("Column permissions", "User A cannot directly update their own row", denied(own), describe(own));
  }
  const base = { owner_id: A.id, platform: "google_maps", source_url: SOURCE_URL };
  const place = await rest("POST", "select=*", A.token, { ...base, name: `${PREFIX} place`, google_place_id: "forged-place" });
  check("Column permissions", "User A cannot set google_place_id directly", place.status >= 400, describe(place));
  const idw = await rest("POST", "select=*", A.token, { ...base, name: `${PREFIX} id`, id: "44444444-4444-4444-8444-444444444444" });
  check("Column permissions", "User A cannot choose a row id directly", idw.status >= 400, describe(idw));
  const ts = await rest("POST", "select=*", A.token, { ...base, name: `${PREFIX} time`, created_at: "2000-01-01T00:00:00Z" });
  check("Column permissions", "User A cannot choose created_at directly", ts.status >= 400, describe(ts));
} finally {
  const like = `name=like.${encodeURIComponent(`${PREFIX}*`)}`;
  await rest("DELETE", `select=*&${like}`, A.token);
  await rest("DELETE", `select=*&${like}`, B.token);
  const left = (await rest("GET", `select=id&${like}`, A.token)).rows.length + (await rest("GET", `select=id&${like}`, B.token)).rows.length;
  check("Cleanup", "All test rows were deleted", left === 0, `${left} row(s) left`);
}

let commit = "unknown"; try { commit = execSync("git rev-parse --short HEAD").toString().trim(); } catch {}
console.log(`### S1-011 live verification\n\nCommit \`${commit}\`, ${new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10)}. IDs are masked.\n`);
let group = "";
for (const c of results) {
  if (c.group !== group) { group = c.group; console.log(`\n**${group}**\n\n| Check | Result | Observed |\n| --- | --- | --- |`); }
  console.log(`| ${c.name} | ${c.pass ? "PASS" : "**FAIL**"} | ${c.actual} |`);
}
const failed = results.filter((c) => !c.pass).length;
console.log(`\n**${results.length - failed} of ${results.length} checks passed.**`);
process.exit(failed ? 1 : 0);
EOF
cat > $SP/pg-supabase.mjs <<'EOF'
// Local test harness only: Supabase-shaped HTTP over real PostgreSQL, enforcing real grants and RLS per user.
import { createServer } from "node:http";
import { execFile } from "node:child_process";
const DB = process.argv[2];
const users = { "a@test.local": "11111111-1111-4111-8111-111111111111", "b@test.local": "22222222-2222-4222-8222-222222222222" };
const b64 = (o) => Buffer.from(JSON.stringify(o)).toString("base64url");
const jwt = (sub) => { const now = Math.floor(Date.now()/1000); return `${b64({alg:"HS256",typ:"JWT"})}.${b64({sub,aud:"authenticated",role:"authenticated",iat:now,exp:now+3600})}.sig`; };
const sub = (t) => { try { const s = JSON.parse(Buffer.from(t.split(".")[1],"base64url")).sub; return Object.values(users).includes(s) ? s : null; } catch { return null; } };
const userObj = (id) => ({ id, aud:"authenticated", role:"authenticated", email:"x@test.local", app_metadata:{}, user_metadata:{}, created_at:"2026-01-01T00:00:00Z" });
const lit = (s) => `'${String(s).replace(/'/g,"''")}'`;
const psql = (sql) => new Promise((res) => execFile("psql", ["--no-psqlrc","-q","-At","-v","ON_ERROR_STOP=1","-d",DB,"-c",sql], (e,out,err) => res({ err: e ? err : null, out })));
async function run(uid, stmt) {
  const claims = uid ? JSON.stringify({ sub: uid, role: "authenticated" }) : "";
  const role = uid ? "authenticated" : "anon";
  const r = await psql(`begin; set local role ${role}; select set_config('request.jwt.claims', ${lit(claims)}, true); ${stmt}; commit;`);
  if (r.err) return { err: r.err };
  const lines = r.out.trim().split("\n"); return { rows: JSON.parse(lines[lines.length-1] || "[]") };
}
createServer(async (req,res) => {
  const url = new URL(req.url,"http://x");
  const send = (s,b) => { res.writeHead(s,{"Content-Type":"application/json"}); res.end(JSON.stringify(b)); };
  const chunks=[]; for await (const c of req) chunks.push(c);
  const body = chunks.length ? JSON.parse(Buffer.concat(chunks).toString()) : null;
  const token = (req.headers.authorization||"").replace(/^Bearer /i,"");
  if (url.pathname==="/ready") return send(200,{});
  if (url.pathname==="/auth/v1/token") { const id = users[body.email]; if (!id||body.password!=="pw") return send(400,{code:"invalid_credentials",message:"no"}); const now=Math.floor(Date.now()/1000); return send(200,{access_token:jwt(id),refresh_token:"r",token_type:"bearer",expires_in:3600,expires_at:now+3600,user:userObj(id)}); }
  if (url.pathname==="/auth/v1/user") { const id=sub(token); return id?send(200,userObj(id)):send(401,{code:"bad_jwt",message:"no"}); }
  if (url.pathname!=="/rest/v1/analysis_targets") return send(404,{message:"unsupported"});
  const uid = sub(token);
  const where = [];
  const id = url.searchParams.get("id"); if (id) where.push(`id = ${lit(id.replace(/^eq\./,""))}::uuid`);
  const name = url.searchParams.get("name"); if (name) where.push(name.startsWith("like.") ? `name like ${lit(name.slice(5).replace(/\*/g,"%"))}` : `name = ${lit(name.replace(/^eq\./,""))}`);
  const w = where.length ? `where ${where.join(" and ")}` : "";
  const lim = url.searchParams.get("limit") ? `limit ${Number(url.searchParams.get("limit"))}` : "";
  const J = (cte) => `with r as (${cte}) select coalesce(json_agg(r),'[]') from r`;
  let stmt;
  if (req.method==="GET") stmt = J(`select * from public.analysis_targets ${w} ${lim}`);
  else if (req.method==="DELETE") stmt = J(`delete from public.analysis_targets ${w} returning *`);
  else if (req.method==="PATCH") { const sets = Object.entries(body).map(([k,v])=>`${k} = ${lit(v)}`).join(", "); stmt = J(`update public.analysis_targets set ${sets} ${w} returning *`); }
  else if (req.method==="POST") { const cols=Object.keys(body); stmt = J(`insert into public.analysis_targets (${cols.join(",")}) select ${cols.join(",")} from json_populate_record(null::public.analysis_targets, ${lit(JSON.stringify(body))}) returning *`); }
  const out = await run(uid, stmt);
  if (out.err) { const denied=/permission denied|row-level security/.test(out.err); return send(denied?(uid?403:401):400,{code:denied?"42501":"23514",message:"database rejected"}); }
  if ((req.headers.accept||"").includes("vnd.pgrst.object")) return out.rows.length===1?send(req.method==="POST"?201:200,out.rows[0]):send(406,{code:"PGRST116",message:"not one row"});
  return send(req.method==="POST"?201:200,out.rows);
}).listen(54331,"127.0.0.1");
EOF
cat > $SP/run-harness.sh <<'EOF'
#!/bin/bash
# usage: run-harness.sh <label> <migration file>
SP=$(dirname "$0"); label=$1; mig=$2; db=s1011_live_$label
export PATH="/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@16/bin:$PATH"
dropdb --if-exists "$db" 2>/dev/null; createdb "$db"
psql --no-psqlrc -q -d "$db" -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -f tests/db/supabase-auth-shim.sql >/dev/null 2>&1
psql --no-psqlrc -q -d "$db" -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -f "$mig" >/dev/null 2>&1
psql --no-psqlrc -q -d "$db" -c "insert into auth.users values ('11111111-1111-4111-8111-111111111111'),('22222222-2222-4222-8222-222222222222')"
node "$SP/pg-supabase.mjs" "$db" > "$SP/pg.log" 2>&1 & FP=$!
export NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:54331 NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY=dummy-publishable-key NEXT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=1
node node_modules/next/dist/bin/next dev --hostname 127.0.0.1 --port 3102 > "$SP/next-$label.log" 2>&1 & NP=$!
for i in $(seq 1 60); do curl -sf -o /dev/null http://127.0.0.1:3102/api/health && break; perl -e 'select(undef,undef,undef,1)'; done
S1011_APP_URL=http://127.0.0.1:3102 S1011_USER_A_EMAIL=a@test.local S1011_USER_A_PASSWORD=pw S1011_USER_B_EMAIL=b@test.local S1011_USER_B_PASSWORD=pw node "$SP/verify-live.mjs"; echo "script exit=$?"
kill $NP $FP 2>/dev/null; pkill -f 'next dev --hostname 127.0.0.1 --port 3102' 2>/dev/null; perl -e 'sleep 2'
echo "rows left in db: $(psql --no-psqlrc -At -d "$db" -c 'select count(*) from public.analysis_targets')"; dropdb "$db"
echo "--- app log (errors)"; grep -E 'Analysis target operation|Error' "$SP/next-$label.log" | cut -c1-150 | sort | uniq -c | head -5
EOF
chmod +x $SP/run-harness.sh
cd "[REDACTED LOCAL REPOSITORY PATH]"; echo '########## FINAL MIGRATION'; $SP/run-harness.sh final supabase/migrations/20261103120000_create_analysis_targets.sql 2>&1 | cut -c1-170; git status --short | wc -l
````

OUT

````text
########## FINAL MIGRATION
### S1-011 live verification

Commit `76fccb2`, 2026-11-06. IDs are masked.


**Unauthenticated**

| Check | Result | Observed |
| --- | --- | --- |
| POST with no session and a spoofed owner returns 401 | PASS | HTTP 401, AUTH_REQUIRED |
| That request created no row | PASS | 0 rows expected |
| POST with an invalid cookie returns 401 | PASS | HTTP 401 |
| Direct anonymous read is denied | PASS | HTTP 401, 0 row(s), code 42501 |
| Direct anonymous insert is denied | PASS | HTTP 401, 0 row(s), code 42501 |

**Validation and errors**

| Check | Result | Observed |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Invalid input returns 400 with field issues | PASS | HTTP 400, INVALID_ANALYSIS_TARGET_INPUT |
| Invalid input created no row | PASS | 0 rows expected |
| Error responses expose no sensitive details | PASS | 400 and 401 bodies scanned |
| Responses are private and not cached | PASS | private, no-cache, no-store, must-revalidate, max-age=0 |

**Ownership**

### Claude

**Yes, you still need steps 1–4, because they're the only way to check your real Supabase project.** Everything I just ran was on my local Postgres, so it can't tell us what's deployed. Step 5 is what changes: use this script instead of the browser-console steps. It covers everything on Neel's list that I can test live.

## Your steps
1. **Check what's deployed:** run the `to_regclass` query in the Supabase SQL editor, as in your paste.
2. **Check the schema or apply the migration:**
   - If `target_table` has a value, run the schema check. If any rows are `false`, send them to me before changing anything.
   - If it's empty, run the migration file from `main`, then run the schema check. All 17 rows should be `true`.
3. **Set up `.env.local`:** copy `.env.example`, then add the project URL and publishable key, not the service-role key. Also add two confirmed test accounts:
   ```
   S1011_USER_A_EMAIL=
   S1011_USER_A_PASSWORD=
   S1011_USER_B_EMAIL=
   S1011_USER_B_PASSWORD=
   ```
4. **Start the app:** on `feature/S1-011-owned-records`, run `npm run dev`. If it's already running, restart it so it picks up `.env.local`.
5. **Run the script:** in a second terminal, from the repo root:
   ```
   node [REDACTED SCRATCHPAD PATH]
   ```
6. **Check the logs:** look at the `npm run dev` terminal for any "Analysis target operation failed" lines, or for emails or tokens. On a clean run there should be no such failure lines.
7. **Send me the results:** that means the query results from steps 1–2 and the script's table. If the script prints any `FAIL`, send that too and we'll sort it out before anything goes on the PR. If it all passes, I'll post the results to #40 for Neel and Shah, then mark it ready.
8. **Clean up:** remove the `S1011_*` lines from `.env.local`.

The script creates a few rows named `S1-011 live check ...` in the shared project and deletes them at the end. It prints a failed check if any are left, along with the name to delete by hand.

## What the script covers
| Neel's check | How it's covered |
|---|---|
| Deployed RLS and column permissions match | Schema check, plus real attempts to get around them |
| Ownership comes only from the session | Script: stored `owner_id` is read back for both users |
| Spoofed owner IDs have no effect | Script: both users send the other's ID |
| Signed-out requests can't create records | Script: no session, a bad cookie and direct API attempts |
| Cross-user access is denied | Script: User B tries to read, update and delete User A's row, and insert a row as User A |
| No sensitive details in errors or logs | Script scans 400 and 401 responses; you check the logs in step 6 |

**Not tested live:** the generic 500 for a database failure, because I can't safely force one on the shared project. That case is covered by the unit tests and my local run, so Neel should know that when reviewing.

## How I tested the script
I ran it locally against real Postgres with real RLS, through a small stand-in for Supabase's API. Real Supabase may respond a little differently, so a surprising `FAIL` could be the script's fault rather than a security problem.

| Local setup | Result |
|---|---|
| Final migration | 24 of 24 passed, no rows left behind |
| Older migration | 20 of 24; the 4 failures were all column permission checks, so it catches that version |
| Handler deliberately trusting a spoofed owner | RLS blocked every write, nothing was saved, and the script reported failures |

The last row shows the two layers of protection working independently: even with a bug in the handler, the database refused the spoofed owner. No repo files changed in this round, and #40 is unchanged.

open(sp+'/comment.md','w').write(c); print('comment chars',len(c))
EOF
perl -e 'sleep 25'; for i in $(seq 1 24); do n=$(gh pr view 40 --json statusCheckRollup --jq '[.statusCheckRollup[]|select(.conclusion==null or .conclusion=="")]|length'); [ "$n" = 0 ] && break; perl -e 'sleep 15'; done
FAILS=$(gh pr view 40 --json statusCheckRollup --jq '[.statusCheckRollup[]|select(.conclusion!="SUCCESS")]|length'); echo "non-success checks: $FAILS"
if [ "$FAILS" = 0 ]; then gh pr edit 40 --body-file $SP/pr-final.md --add-reviewer neel-0064,shahdp2 2>&1 | tail -1; gh pr comment 40 --body-file $SP/comment.md 2>&1 | tail -1; gh pr ready 40 2>&1 | tail -1; else echo 'NOT POSTING: CI not green'; fi
gh pr view 40 --json isDraft,mergeable,mergeStateStatus,reviewDecision,headRefOid,changedFiles,closingIssuesReferences,statusCheckRollup,reviewRequests --jq '"draft=\(.isDraft) \(.mergeable)/\(.mergeStateStatus) review=\(.reviewDecision) head=\(.headRefOid[0:7]) files=\(.changedFiles) closes=\([.closingIssuesReferences[].number]) requested=\([.reviewRequests[].login])", (.statusCheckRollup[]|.name+": "+(.conclusion//.status))'; git status --short | wc -l
````
